UG programs:

A total of five (5) Girl Students, the Top four

between 1 and 10000 and the Top one (1) Girl
student who achieves ACPC Rank between 1
and 50, will be eligible for reimbursement of
the tuition fees for the First semester.

PG programs:

The curriculum revision of our UG programme
is typically carried out in S years and for PG
programmes the time frame is 3 years. To
undertake this task, a Curriculum Revision
Committee is constituted, which collects
inputs through surveys and feedback from
faculty, students, alumni, and external
experts. The committee then prepares a
comprehensive report and presents it to the
Board of Studies (Bo).

Students are provided the flexibility to choose

Science electives - designed to promote

Open electives - broadens perspectives

The academic progression of all programs of
DAU is conducted continuously through
discussions in the Under Graduate (UG) and

the University.

Ideas and suggestions arising out of such
deliberations are minuted and brought before
the Board of Studies (BoS)} and Academie

Based on the approval from BoS and AC, any
course curriculum may undergo changes vis a

practical components, method of evaluation
etc. Meetings of the BoS and AC are organized
regularly.
The University has constituted its Internal
Quality Assurance Cell (IOAC) which assesses
academic performance of the University and
SURRESLS appropriate modifications.

IQAC looks after the appropriateness of the
infrastructure for smooth running of the
University.

Any modification suggested by IQAC is
implemented by the offices of Dean-AP and
the Registrar

| 3.10 | Does the University have a continuous
internal evaluation system?

Yes, all programmes offered at DAU follow
continuous evaluation methods such as
Quizzes & Class Tests, Assignments, lab Work,
Tutorials, Class/ Lab Presentations, Projects
_Mini-projects, Viva-voce / Oral Exams.

The question papers are set on the basis of
the course outline and course content,
grading policy, weightage assigned to
different components of the course, which
are being announced to students by the
course instructor in the beginning of the
semester.

The Course Instructor who conducts classes
and is in charge of the course sets the
question paper. Confidentiality is strictly
maintained, and paper-setting is poverned by
internal “sealed cover" protocols. Invigilators
consist of faculty members, supported by
staff or TAs. They are responsible for
maintaining discipline in the exam hall,
ensuring exam protocols (ID verification, no
malpractice) and submitting attendance and
reports to the Examination Cell,

For details of the question paper setters,
board of examinations and invigilators, refer
to Annexure XVI.

mechanism for attracting and recruiting the |
-Whether selection committee was] qualified faculty.

constituted as per the UGC Regulation? « Stage 1: Dean(Faculty) does pre-screening of |
all applications based on eligibility criteria. A |
PhD from a reputed university is the |
minimum qualification, In very exceptional
cases, this requirement is relaxed
(particularly, for Law and Design programs).
After pre-screening, the Dean(Faculty)
forwards a filtered set of applications to the
Area coordinator for reviewing each
application through area members.

« Stage 2: Area coordinator collate feedback of
Area members on each application, convencs
a meeting with the faculty recruitment
members of the Area. The recruitment
committee of the Area members reviews
each candidate's profile and decides whether
to shortlist a candidate to the final round or
nat.

* Stage 3: Shortlisted candidates are invited for
the interview by the Selection Committee, |
comprising external experts, Director, |
Dean(Faculty, Dean[Academic Programs) and |
internal faculty recruitment members. |

Te ee ee . 1

Accredited with an At Grade by NAAC in 7023, een

Designated as a Centre of Excellence by the Gavernmen |
of Gujarat, initially for the period 2022-2025, an
subsequently extended from 2025 ta 2031.

Under the Gujarat State Institutional Rating Framewo
(GSIRF), which began assessing universities in 2023, the
university earned the highest 5-Star rating in both 202
and 2024 [latest). |

High-Quality Students and Faculty

The university consistently attracts meritorious _
from across India, with JEE closing ranks around 10,

for some undergraduate programs and between 16,000—
22,000 for others. It remains a preferred destination for
aspirants seeking admission to B, Tech programs in ICT,
MINC, and EVD. The ACPC closing ranks based on GUJCE
for some programs it is below 100, and for others, the
are between 150 and 225.

University

UGC Proforma for Inspection

« The university also draws high-calibre faculty membe
with outstanding academic and research credentials.
Nearly all hold Ph.D. degrees from prestigiou
institutions in India and abroad,

ioneer in Interdisciplinary Programs

* Since its inception in 2001, the university has been 3
pioneer in offering the B. Tech program in Information
and Communication Technology (ICT}—a distinctive and
interdisciplinary program integrating computer science,
electronics and VLSI design, communication
engineering, and humanities & social sciences. Over the
years, the program has established a strong reputation,
consistently attracting high-achleving students who exce
as professionals and scholars, a tradition that continues
to this day.

« Building on this legacy, the university has introduced
several other interdisciplinary programs, including M.Sc
in Data Science (2020), M.Sc. in Agriculture Analytics |
joint program offered with two partner institutes, 2022),
B.Tech. in Mathematics and Computing (2020), and
B.Tech. in Electronics and VLSI Design (2023).

« The curricula of all programs are regularly revised to
remain aligned with emerging industry trends and
requiraments, ensuring academic mgor and
contemporary relevance.

~

ae Scholarships
The university is committed to ensuring that no admitted,
student is denied the opportunity to pursue education
| due to financial constraints. To uphold this cammit ment,
it offers a range of scholarships to both undergraduate
and postgraduate students, based on merit and merit-
CuMm-means criteria.
Under the merit-cum-means category, students may,
receive full tuition fee waiver, or partial walvers of |
or 50% of the tuition fee.
A significant proportion of students benefit from financial
support in the form of scholarships, stipends, and
fellowships, amounting to % 5 crores during FY 2024-25.
For F¥ 2025-26, this allocation has been substantially
increased to ® 8.4 crores to extend the benefit te a fa: Ber

I]
]
|
| oe of deserving students.

eaching, Learning gf & Research

The university emphasizes learning through research an

hands-on project work. A significant number o
undergraduate research projects have been published in
reputed international journals. To further encourag

scholarly pursuits, the university has instituted a Researe

Excellence Award for students.

At the end of their first year, all 8. Tech student

undertake a mandatery three-week Rural Internship,
Program, designed to expose them to real-world
challenges in rural communities and encourage them ta
propose practical solutions. This experience helps instill
social awareness and a sense of responsibility beyond
the campus environment.

The university hosts 22 active student clubs spanning
cultural, sports, and techno-cultural activities. These
clubs offer students ample opportunities to pursue their
interests and enhance soft skills, complementing their
academic development,

To nurture a vibrant research culture, the itiesiiy
offers Seed Grants, Cumulative Professional
Development Allowance (CPDA), Research Incentives
such as awards for high-quality publications, funding
support for conference participation and funding for
organising workshops in emerging areas. The teachin

load is deliberately kept low—two courses in on

semester and one in the other—to promote high- “quality,
research engagement. Several faculty members also!
maintain active academic and research collaborations
with universities and institutions both within India and
OVeErsedas.

The faculty members are research-intensive, regularly
publishing in high-quality international journals and
conferences. Many have secured competitive research
grants from agencies such as SERB, DoT, ISRO, DRDO,
NBHM, ANRF, and GUICOST. Recently, the universit

received CSR funding exceeding £13 crores to establish a
Smart Energy Learning Centre (SELC), focused on energ

efficiency and renewable energy research. Around 2

faculty members are currently working on 17
interdisciplinary research projects applying Al, ML, Dat

Science, Electronics, Communication, and Robotics t
address challenges in the energy domain. The total

stands at 323.9 crores.

+ The institute is proud to host one of the finest technical
libraries in the region, housing 36,025 printed books,
subscriptions to 32,052 e-journals, and 39 print Lert
and magazines. Over the past three years, the library,
expenditure has been %1.63 crores (2022-23), %1.60
crores (2023-24), and %1.72 crores (2024-25), and the

| total cumulative investment till date is 423.39 crores.

Annexure V1

Academic Requirements for the Ph D Program in
Engineering in ICT disciplines

Science and Mathematics
(Effective from Autumn 2024-25)

Doctor of Philosophy: PhD: duration - typically a 4 to 6 years program. The program will be
governed by these rules, subject to amendments, from time to time, as per the needs and
requirements. These rules deal only with the post-admission academic activities of the program
Eligibility for admission, admission procedures etc., for the program are outside the purview of this
document.

The Dean (Academic Programs)/Registrar may, from time to lime, issue such instructions or
directions as may be necessary to give effect to and carry out the provisions of these rules, The
Director, as Chairman of the Academic Council, may relax/exempt some provision(s) of the rules
in éxceptional situations and all such cases shall be reported to the Academic Council in the
immediate next meeting.

Important terms/expressions used in the document have been defined in the GLOSSARY at the
end of this document.

1, REGISTRATION
1.1 Categories of Registration:

a) A student may register in a given semester in three possible categories: Regular (Full-time)
registration, Sponsored (Part-time) registration, and External (Part-time) registration. Only
resident registration will count towards the residence requirement for a Degree. A foreign
student on a student visa will not be allowed to register a5 an external student

b) To qualify for regular (resident) category registration, the student must be residential, a
minimum of 3 years from the time of first semester registration for a duly approved course
programme and pay the prescribed tuition and other fees, including any outstanding dues. The
sludent must carry his/her coursework and research work at the Institute.

c) Incase a student wants to shift from regular category to part-time category after 3 years due
to any reason, his/her supervisor, after seeking the approval from his/her RPC, should
recommend the same to Dean (AP). The supervisor then sends a formal letter to the Dean AP
office recommending the transition from full-time to part-time category. The approval from RPC
can be obtained over email. Such a request can be made only if the minimum course credits
(as per Table 1 below) have been eamed by the student,

d) To qualify for sponsored (part-time) and external (part-lime) category registration. a slucent
must register for a duly approved research programme, and pay the preg ALF nity
other fees, including any oulstanding dues. i

e) A student is required to register in the semester in which the final Ph
evaluation and pay the prescribed tuition and other fees, including an

1.2 New entrants to the program, who are awaiting the results of the qualifying examination may
be allowed ‘provisional registration. Latest by the date given in the Academic Calendar
(usually about 6 weeks from (he date of registration) such candidates will be required to submit,
for verification, the Certificates of having passed the qualifying examination. Orginal
certilicates will be returned to the students and a self- attested copy will be kept for records.

1.3 Late Registration:

a) If for any compelling reason like illness, a student is unable to register on the day of
registration, he/she will be allowed to register during the late registration period as specified
in the Academic Calendar (which is about one week from the date of registration). Any student
registering late will be required {o pay the specified late registration fee.

b) In exceptional cases, the Dean (Academic Programs) on the recommendation of the Post-
Graduate Committee (PGC) may consider registration beyond the date of late registration. In
such a case, the student will be allowed to register for thesis credits only.

1.4 Academic Advising:

a) Course work:
(i) The academics at DA-IICT is divided into seven areas: Communication and Signal Processing
(CSP), Computer Science (CS), Electronics and VLSI (EnV), Physics (Phy), Mathematics
(Math), Humanities, Arts & Social Science (HSS) and Design.

(ii) The full time students pursuing research in any of the above areas need to register for a
minimum of three courses (Sxx or 7xx only) during the first semester. Out of the three, students
have to register for two area core courses, whose list is made available by (he PhD coordinaior.
The two core courses offered by an area need to be done by all the students registered in the
PhD program of that area. In addition to the two core courses, the student needs to register
for the compulsory Research Methodology course. Regular PhD candidates are allowed to
lake a maximum of one MOOC to fulfil their minimum course credit requirements throughout
the entire PhD program (this MOOC should be the Research Methodology course)

(iii) The core courses will be informed by the PhD coordinator during the orientation program,
Students are recommended to get in touch with the area coordinators for specific questions
on the area core courses, For registering one more course other than the 3 courses in the firs!
semester, or for registering for courses from semester || onward, students are recommended
io discuss with the Area-Coordinator/PhO Coordinator/PG-Convenor/Any other faculty
member while making a choice. After the supervisor appointment is done, students are
recommended to discuss the possible choices of the courses they would like to register with
the supervisor

(iv) The part-time students pursuing research in any of the above areas need to register for a
minimum of three PG-level courses during the first semester, out of which one is the
compulsory Research Methodology course. The other two courses need to be decided in
consultations with the supervisor. A course can be done through any one of the three modes
below

Mode 1: By registering for the course and its exam on any UGC recognized platform such as
NPTEL, etc., and obtaining the passing certificate which contains performance in all the
evaluation components. A, 2/3-credits (2-0- 0-2/3- 0-0- “3) course must be of a minimum duration

and possible grades in our system is given in section 2.4.

Mode 2: By registering for the course (Sxx/7xx, or 4xx if offered for PG af
the course is offered for UG/PG students in the current semester, and|
and lab sessions in person. This is possible for students having jobs oNete
nature, or it is feasible for them to do so alongwith the jab.

Mode 3: By registering for the course (5xx/7xx, or 4xx if offered for PG students) on-campus if the
course is offered for UG/PG students in the current semester, but doing it in online mode
(provided the instructor agrees to conduct it in online mode). The instructor shall conduct
separate interaction/lecture sessions and the student will appear for all the evaluation
components in person along with the other UG/PG students registered in the course as per
the course policy.

{v) The full-time students can also take a course in Mode 1 as above on the recommendation
of the supervisor and with prior approval of the PhD coordinator, However, only one such
course per semester can be taken, The credits will be counted towards the overall credits once
the passing certificate which contains all the details as mentioned in Mode 1 is submitted to
the PhD coordinator.

(vi) Under all the three categories of registration (full-time, part-time and sponsored), the
minimum course credits can be earned until registering for the synopsis.

b) Supervisor(s) Assignment (Full-Time category)

(i) During Semester |, students are expected to discuss and identify a prospective thesis
supervisor(s). Subsequently, after semester |,a supervisor in the relevant research area
will be appointed by the Dean(AP)'s office for the student after receiving a formal request
from the student and the prospective thesis supervisor(s),

(ii) The thesis supervisor must be finalized before the student appears for the Part || of the
comprehensive examination. Once the thesis supervisor is finalized, the RPC will be formed
by the office of Dean-AP in consultation with the supervisor

{iii} To complete the Comprehensive Part-ll by the end of Semester Il, the supervisor
election/appointment must be done within the first two months from the start of the semester,
ie, if students’ semester Il is the Winter semester of the academic year, then the supervisor
selection and appointment must be done by the end of February, while if semester Il is the
Autumn semester of the academic year, then the supervisor selection and appointment must
be done by the middle of September. More details about comprehensive exams are provided
in section 5

c) Supervisor(s) Assignment (Part-Time category)
At the start of semester |, the faculty member whose letter of recommendation (LoR) the
student submits al the time of admission, is appointed as the supervisor,

d) A student may be permitted to repeat or substitute courses in which he/she has obtained ‘DD,
‘DE’ or ‘F' grades. Permission to repeat/substitute a course will be governed by the guidelines laid
down in section 2.4.

1.5 Semester Load Requirements:

a) A student registered for a full semester load solely by course work would typically take 3/4
courses. Depending on the merits of the case, the PGC may permit a student to register for a
maximum of 5 courses on the recommendation of the PhD coordinator and thesis- SupeIviEsr
(if appointed). A minimum of 3 courses needs to be registered in any semester
load is typically defined as equivalent to 12 credits, If the minimum researg f
is fulfilled, then the students are advised to register for only 3 research goats

for a maximum of 12 credits or a minimum of 3 credits during the regulq

Regula r (Full-time) —

Adding/Dropping of Courses and Withdrawal from a Semester

Adding and dropping of courses is permitted, during the Add/Drop period, only if the student's
request is endorsed by the instructor of the course and the Convener PGC. The last dates of
applying for adding and dropping of courses are specified in the Academic Calendar.

4 student who wishes to withdraw prior to registration for a semester must obtain a formal
approval from the Dean (Academic Programs) before the prescribed last date for late
registration for the concerned semester. Withdrawal after registration for a semester is
permitted only on medical grounds or for other exceptional reasons and formal approval for
such withdrawal must be obtained from the Dean (Academic Programs) before the date of
commencement of the end-semester examination for the concerned semester. Withdrawal
from a semester, either prior to registration or affer registration, is permitted for only one
semester at a time. If a student does not register for a regular semester or does not withdraw
with permission from the Dean (Academic Programs) as indicated above, his/her name is
liable to be struck off from the rolls of the Institute.

A sludent who registers for a semester after having withdrawn in previous semester(s) can
register for the available courses as prescribed in the curriculurn for that particular semester
subpect to pre-requisites, if any.

The transcript of a student who has ‘withdrawn’ status would show the appropriate status for
the concerned semester(s). The transcript of a student who is suspended for an academic or
disciplinary reason would also show ‘withdrawn’ status.

The maximum period for completion of the program as given in the appropriate subsection of
Section 2 includes any semester in which the student has ‘wilhdrawn' status.

In case a Student has registered for a course on a UGC recognised platfonm such as NPTEL
in a particular semester and is unable to obtain a completion certificate, the status of such a
course willbe shown “Withdrawn (WY on the marksheet! in that semester,

2.1.2 Research credits (Example: PC801-Research-1 (3 crediis)) are advisable for the student
once he/she successfully clears the comprehensive examination, The number of research
courses a student can register in a semester is the same as the number of courses (i.e., min
3 courses and max. 4 courses). Depending on the merits of the case, the PGC may permita
student to register for a maximum of 5 courses on the recommendation of the PhD
coordinator and thesis-supervisor (if appointed). The students are required to register the
Synopsis as one Research Unit (3 credits).

2.1.3 Courses of level 1 (Le., 1xx), 2 (ie., 2xx), 3 (.@., Sex) and 6 (Le., 6xx) won't count for the

credit, and extra academic components could be added to such courses, to elevate them at
par with the PhD level course work. Such a permissible course will have a different course
identity for the PhO student's registration.

2.1.4 A student eams 3-credits for every self-study course taken. Maximum number of allowed
self-study courses throughout the PhD is two with no more than one in a single semester,
Self-study courses can be taken from semester Il onward (i.e., no self-study course in
semester |), with no more than one self-study course under the same instructor. For taking a

The student's performance on his/her self-study course would be evaluated by the course
instructor with whom the student is doing the self-study course. A letter grade would be
awarded by the instructor of the student based on the student's performance in the self-study
course. The grade has to be communicated to the PhD-coordinator.

2.2 Audit Courses:

The students are permitted to audit courses. They will be given a ‘P’ grade, which will be entered
in {hear grade card if they satisfy the requirements placed by the course instructor. If they do not
meet the requirements, then they will not get any grade and no entry will be made in the grade-
card/transcript for that course.

or on the recommendation of the supervisor, with the prior approval of the Dean (Academic
Programs), For such courses, the credits earned won't count towards the minimum course credits.
However, [hey may be counted in the total minimum credits.

Residency during a semester implies that a student is available on the campus of the Institute for

2.7 PhD Synopsis

A PhD student is expected to successfully complete PhD Synopsis examination by the stipulated
maximum duration, as indicated in Table 1.

A student should obtain consent of his/her supervisor/RPC for synopsis registration.

A student needs to register for synopsis at the beginning of the semester

The Synopsis Committee for the candidate will be constituted by Dean (AP).

The Synopsis presentation must be done by the end of the semester.

After successfully completing the PhD Synopsis exam, the student should submil a PhD
thesis to the Dean (AP) within a maximum time of 6 months

Registration for the synopsis can be done only if the minimum course credits, minimum
research credits and minimum total credits have been eamed by the student.

3. ACADEMIC PERFORMANCE REQUIREMENT

3.1 Semester Performance Index (SPI) and Cumulative Performance Index (CPI):

The SPI is an indicator of the academic performance of a student in all the courses he/she has
registered during a given semester. It is computed by taking the weighted average of the grades
obtgined in that semester. The CP! indicates the cumulative academic performance in all the
courses taken including those taken in the current semester. CPI is computed by taking the
cumulative weighted average of the grades earned till that semester. The SP! and CPI is calculated
up to two decimal places. Courses with ‘S’ and ‘x’ will not be taken into account in the above
comnulations.

3.2 Minimum CPI requirements for graduation in the program:

| Program | CPI for Graduation

Pho | 7.0

3.3 Academic Probation and Dismissal:

A student whose CPI falls below the minimum required for graduation ai the end of any semester
will be placed on Academic Probation for the next semester with written intimation. A student in
the Ph D program will be placed on Academic Probation if he/she obtains an % in a research
course. For every student placed on Academic Probation fora semester, the PGC will prescribe a
specified course load in the concerned semester and may also prescribe a minimum SP! the
student must attain in the semester. The PGC will keep a watch on the progress of every student
placed on probation and if the performance of a student is poor so that he/she ts not likely to benefit
from continuing in the program any further, will recommend to the Director that he/she should leave
the Institute. A student who obtains more than 5 unsatisfactory (x) grades shall be discontinued
from the program. If a student's continuation in the program is terminated, the appropriate authonty
will issue the letter of termination.

4. FELLOWSHIPS/TEACHING ASSISTANTSHIPS

(stipend) in the form of Teaching Assistantship (TA) / Research AME:
Stipend is for a maximum duration of five years, The responsibilitiecns
teaching / research assistantship includes conducting laboratory couks
undergraduate/postgraduate students. assisting in teaching, research proj

administration. The PhD stipend amount is announced in the Admissions Policy (and available
on the website) of the Institute. A student can clarify stipend related doubts with Dean
(AP\/PGC.

4.3 The stipend would be decreased by 50% for the following semester in case a student obtains
more than one unsatisfactory (%) grade in any semester or the CPI falls below 7/10.

5. COMPREHENSIVE EXAMINATION

5.

—_

Students registered in the program must pass a comprehensive examination designed to test
the overall comprehension of the student in the relevant subjects.

5.2 (i) For Full-time students, the comprehensive examination consists of two parts: (I) Written
or qualifiers (Part |) and (Il) Proposal defense (Part Il), The Part | examination will test a
candidate's competency in the broad discipline, in which the candidate plans to pursue his/her
PhD study. The syllabus of part! of the comprehensive examination will be based on the area
core courses. The part ll,i.@., proposal defense, consists of a literature survey and a research
proposal which must be presented to RPC no later than four months after a candidate clears
the Part |, None of the parts can be conducted before the start of semester || of the student

(ii) The area coordinators will be responsible for organizing the comprehensive examination
Part | for all eligible students every semester, while the supervisor and the RPC of a student
are responsible for organizing the comprehensive examination Part Il.

(iii) The Part | and || examinations can be conducted in tandem.

{iv) For Part-time students, the comprehensive examination consists of Part !i only. The
details of Part ll are the same as thal mentioned above. Part Il cannot be conducted before
ihe start of semester || of the student

5.3 Students should have a minimum CP! of 7.0/10 in order to be eligible for appearing in
either/both of the part | & Il of the comprehensive examination. Also, the student must have
registered for and passed the three courses as per the details mentioned in section 1.4 (a).

9.4 For full-time students, if students’ semester Il is the Winter semester of the academic year,
then the Part | written test will be held at the end of January, with Part |l expected by the end
of May. Allernatively, if semester Il is the Autumn semester of the academic year, then the
Part | written test will be held in the middle of August, with Part Il expected by the middle of
December

5.6 The maximum number of attempts for either of the parts of the comprehensive exam is two. A
student can appear for only one PhD Comprehensive examination (each of the pars) ina
given semester.

5.7 When a student has not passed the comprehensive examination in his/her first attempt, a
second comprehensive examination will be conducted by the same board that was constituted
earlier unless decided otherwise by the Dean (Academic Programs). In case the duration
between the first examination and the desired second one is considered to be short, he/she
may not be allowed to appear for the second examination.

afier he/she has passed the comprehensive examination (both thal fy
comprehensive examination shall imply passing both the parts for the full\tigge
passing Part || for the part-time students. sc

6. Supervisor Selection and RPC Formation

Supervisor for a student enrolled in the PhD program under sponsored and Part-lime calegones
is the same person whose LoR the student submits at the time of admission, The Dean (AP)
formally assigns the student with the supervisor by the middle of semester 1. In exceptional
circumstances where the faculty member who provided the Letter of Recommendation (Lor) is
unavailable to supervise the student, a new PhD supervisor will be appointed through the
appropriate procedural framework.

Primary thesis supervisor for a student enrolled in the PhD program under the regular category
can be identified by the student as early as possible, preferably by the end of the first semester
Once a prospective PhD thesis supervisor is identified, the Dean (AP) can formally assign the
student with the supervisor after Semester |. The supervisor appointment must be done before the
student appears for the part Il of the comprehensive exam.

Research Progress Committee (RPC) comprising the thesis supervisor and two facully members
in the relevant research area will be formed by the Dean(AP}'s office for the candidate after the
supervisor appointment. Out of the two RPC members, one external expert can be included with
the approval of Dean-AP.

7. Research Progress Seminar

Research Progress Seminar (RPS) is held twice a year, (typically once in November (for Autumn
Semester), and once in April (for Winter Semester) for the students who have clearec their
comprehensive examination (both the parts).

« RPC of the student will assess the student's research progress.

- RPC may invite an external expert during the student's RPS. The supervisor of the student
requires a formal approval of the Dean(AP) for involving an extemal expert in his/her
student's RPS.

- Grades of the student will be decided by the supervisor in consultations with the RPC
members.

8. MIGRATION RULES

8.1 Eligibility for M Tech Degree: A Ph D student who fails to pass the Ph D comprehensive
examination within the specified duration, whether admitted directly or via internal migration
is eligible to receive the M Tech degree under the following conditions
: The student fulfils the eligibility criteria for M Tech program
- The student fulfils the criteria for continuation in the M Tech program
: The student submits an M Tech thesis which fulfils the requirements for such within a

maximum of two semesters. This duration would commence from the semester
immediately following the semester in which the student failed the Ph D comprehensive
examination. Furthermore, the student would not be eligible for financial support during
this perod.

8.2 Ph D to M Tech Migration: A student in the Ph B program is eligible to migrate to the M Tech

Ph D and fulfils the following critena:
- The students should have entered the program with a BTech/BE deg
- An appropriate M Tech program should be available in the Institute. \
* — Atleast 18 course credits completed.
: The student should have a minimum CPI of 6.5/10.0.

#.3 Completion of Requirements for M Tech Program: A student who migrates to the M Tech
program from the Ph D program must complete all requirements for the M Tech Degree within
twa years (four semesters) from the time of migration. However, credits earned as a Ph D
student would be carried over to the M Tech program.

9. Termination of a student's PhO Admission

* More than 5 unsatisfactory(X) grade in research courses during the entire PhD program
would lead to a tenmination.

* Student who has not defended his/her PhD Synopsis successfully before the ‘Max
Duration (Years) allowed then admission of that student will be terminated fram the PhD
program. Such a student may request for fresh admission to the PhD program by
submitting an applicaton to the Admission Committee. If the Admission Committee
approves his/her admission, the candidate can be registered as a fresh PhD student and
he/she will require completing academic requirements.

* Ifthe student fails to pass the comprehensive examination even in the second attempt,
then his/her PhD admission will get terminated.

10. GLOSSARY

Academic Probation: Academic Probation indicates that a student's academic performance is
notup tothe expected level. Over and above the academic consequences described in section
3.4, a student who has been placed on probation at any time may be subjected to other
restrictions related to financial support, award of medals and prizes, etc.

Cumulative Performance Index (CPI): CPI indicates the cumulative academic performance in all
the courses taken including those taken in the current semester. CPI is computed by taking
the cumulative weighted average of the grades earned till that semester.

Grade Points: Product of the credits and points of a letter grade awarded to the course.

Postgraduate Committee (PGC): Committee of the Institute responsible for Policy Guidelines
and Implementation Strategies covering the Postgraduate Programs.

Semester: Approximately 16 weeks duration each, the first one (Autumn Semester) extending
from July to November and the second (Winter Semester) from December! January to April.

Semester Credits: The sum of credits of courses registered by the student in a semester.

Semester Grade Points: The sum of the products of credits and points for each course registered
by a student in a semester,

Semester Performance Index (SPI): SPI is an indicator of the academic performance of a student
in all the courses he/she has registered during a given semester. It is computed by taking the
weighted average of the grades obtained in that semester.

Minutes of the meeting held on 10 May 2022 at 4:00 pm — 5:00 pm (virtual mode)

Agenda: MVech(ICT). MYech( EC) curriculum revision

Attendices

Ir. C Jinwala, SWNPT, Surat

Dr. Anirban Daseupia. Tin

Dr. MV Joshi, DA-1CT

lr. Anjan Ghosh, DA-HCT

Dr, Anish Mathuria, DA-IICT

Dr, Yash Vasavada, DA-LCT

Dr. Saurabh Tiwari, PG Convenor, DA-NCT
Dr. Maniklal Das, Dean-Academics, DA-IICT
Dr. KS Dasgupta, Director, DA-1CT

Prof. Dasgupta welcome all,

Prof. Anish Mathura made a detailed presentation with the philosophy of the proposed
structure, highlighting about the restricted NMexibility of the existing structure. lack of
intemship opportunity, less number of elective in the respective specialization, The proposed
structure has browght these important components in it, which he had presented in details in
the presentation,

Prof. Saurabh ‘Tiwari made the detailed course structure for Software Systems. Machine
Learning and VLSI & Embedded Systems specializations. Followed by, Prof. Yash Vasavada
shared the detailed courses of Wireless Communication and Signal Processing specialization
for the MTeeh( iC) program.

Following observations/comments/suggestions were shared:

Broadly. members in the committee acknowledged the efforts put in the proposed structure.

Prof, Anirban Dasgupta asked about the intemship provision, whether it is in summer OR in
the +" semester. which was clarified in stating that the internship option is in the 4" semester.

Prof, Devesh Jinwala suggested to check prerequisite of courses, Whether it is good to have
strict compartment for Gen. Elective and Specialization Elective; whether Adv. Siw Engg OR
something like System Specification and Verification kind of course, which were cl: wified i im
the discussion.

P rot. Jinwala = asked abet pros iston of iphone one-year ate liw as clarified in

program objective: otherwise, the proposed structure is a balancing approach for both one-
vear thesis und one semester internship options,

In summary. the proposed revision in the curriculum of the MVech(ICT) and MTeech( EC) was
accepted and appreciated by the members of the committee.

The mecting concluded with acknowledgement and thanks to cveryone.

Minutes of Board of Studies Meeting
Date & Time: 20 June 2023 @ 3:00 PM — 4:00 PM
Venue: Board Room, Girector Office

Agenda:

Curriculum revision of the MSc (IT) Program

Members present in the meeting:

KS Dasgupta, Director

Manikial Das, Dean-AP

Saurabh Tiwari, PG Convenor

Vishvajit Pandya, MDes Program Co-ordinator

Tapas Kumar Maiti, MTech ICT Program Co-ordinator
Yash Vasavada, MTech EC Program Co-ordinator
MV Joshi, Domain Expert

PM Jat, UG Convenor

Devesh Jinwala, Domain Expert, SVNIT

Could nat attend: LY Subramaniam, Domain Expert, IBM IRL; Ranendu Ghosh, MSc AA
Program Co-ordinator.

Director welcomes the members of the board. Dean (AP) briefed about the agenda of the
moeting.

Prof. Saurabh Tiwari shared the review proposal for the MSc 17 currtoulum based on the
inputs provided in the previous meetings by the faculty members and review committee.
After the presentation, the members of the board have the following views:

Prof. Jinwala: The proposal is well thought out and nicely presented. The curriculum is
sound; it addresses the necessary elements that will make students Industry ready. The
proposal includes DevOps as one of the elective courses. However, the execution of the
course is challenging. Several universities have been implementing DevOps tools in different
courses from the first semester for Cl/CD of the projects. Security is one of the elective
courses added to the curriculum. Security aspects such as ethical hacking, security
vulnerabilities, @tc., can be covered,

The students may get exposed to the DevOps tools in the Software Engineering course as a
part of their course project, which may be addressed in Software Engineering course.

Prof. Pandya: Mention the unique selling points of the program, which will attract potential
Students to the MSc IT program.

Dean-AP: The proposal has gone through extensive deliberations with multiple faculty
meetings. The revised curriculum has incorporated inputs af the stakeholders. It there are
no other inputs from the members of board of studies, the proposed curriculum is
considered approved by the board which will be placed before academic council for its
ratification and approval appropriately,

The Director concluded the meeting with thanks ————_

Compiled on 26 June 2023

Item No. 2
To obtain the approval of Chairman and Members, Academic Council to
implement new PG program on MSc in Agriculture Analytics

DA-IICT, in partnership with Indian Institute of Remote Sensing (IRS), ISAO and Anand
Agricultural University (AAU), Anand, has started a two-year MSc degree program in
Agriculture Analytics. The objective of the program is to introduce the concept of
satellite technology and data processing, machine learning, mathematical and statistical
modeling in the program curriculum.

Presentation on Agriculture Analytics program was made by Prof Ranendu Ghosh.
He presented the genesis, need, program structure and current status of the new
program, It is envisaged that the students in the program will move to DAIICT, IAS
and AAU in the 1", 2% and 3“ semesters respectively. One unique feature of the
program is to introduce Value Added Courses (VAC) in the summer semester by the
industrias/academia before they move to AAU for the 3° semester, Or Ghosh also
briefed that a total of 29 students were admitted in the first batch of the program. The
admission process was based on admission test (50% weigtage), academic
credentials (20% weigtage) and interview (30% weigtage). Before the orientation
program at DAIICT, the students were undergone a two days bootcamp during July
18 — 19 2022, This was essentially an interaction program of the students with related
agritech industries.

Observations made by AC members are as follows

+ Participation of the indusiries and their interest on engaging with the students
should bea ensured in the program.

« Prospect of higher studias (PhD) should be explored once they complete the
PG program.

* Encourage the admission of girl students in the program

« Introduce the agriculture policy issues in the curriculum.

The members expressed their satisfaction about the initiative of starting a new
mulidisciplinary and multinstitutional program

ltem No. 3
Presentation by Prof Anish Mathuria on the revised M.Tech ICT program

The objective of the revamped MTech (ICT) curriculum is to develop a flexible
structure that allows students to choose courses according to their interests. The
curriculum also allows them to undertake an internship in the final semester. Changes
to the curriculum include adding a minor project in the second semester and a major
project to be carried out during the summer semester and the following autumn
semester. Students who choose to undertake thesis research would be allowed to
spend the final semester at a collaborating research partner organization.

There were few comments from members of the council as follows
* What is the duration of internship?
« What is the percentage distribution of students in thesis and internship option.

Dean AP responded that percentage is 50 : 50 in the first year and 40 : 60 in the
second year,

Members, in general, commented that intamship is a good option for the students. It
was suggested that for thesis option, the students should work on proble defined by
the industries. ~

The Council endorsed the improvements in the program.

Item No. 4

Presentation by Prof Yash Vasavda on the revised M.Tech EC program jointly
offered by DA-IICT and CR Rao AIMSCS

DA-IICT, in partnership with CR Rao AIMSCS Institute, offers a two-year MTech degree
program in Electronics and Communication (EC) with specialization in Wireless
Communications and Signal Processing (WSP). The objective is to position the
graduating students at the forefront of industrial and academic research developments
in the advanced wireless communication systems and signal processing architectures.

The degree program structure is as follows.

« In their first semester, the students undertake the course work comprising a core
subject (Introduction to Wireless Communication), a Lab course, a general
elective in mathematics and a general elective in technical domain.

« In the second semester, the students take a specialization core || (Advanced
Digital Signal Processing), and two specialization electives. In addition, the
students work on a Minor Project | in the second semester. The students
undertake a Major Project | in the summer semester. In the third semester, the
students study two specialization electives and continue working on the Major
Project |. Finally, in their fourth semester, the students opt to conduct either the
Major project Il on the campus, or undertake an Industrial Training Project. If a
student undertakes the Major Project Il, the program requires it to be a
continuation of Major Project |, Upon successful completion of the Major Project
il, the student is awarded a thesis certificate.

The students are required to be at DA-IICT during the first two semesters: they can opi
to be either at DA-IICT or CR Rao AIMSCS during Semesters Ili and lV.

There were comments as follows
« Students with VLSI but without Signal Processing (SP) knowledge may not be

useful
e Raspberry experience will be useful

Dean AP responded that a course on VLSI with SP can be offered.

Teaching or Research Assistantship is offered, as per the UGC norms, to all the eligible
students. Total intake is 16, with 12 seats reserved for the GATE-qualitied students

After detailed discussion the council members ratified the improvements suggasted by
the institute.

The meeting ended with a vote of thanks to the Chair.

item No. 3
Ta obtain the approval of Chairman and Members, Academic Council to implement
new UG program on BTech in Electronics and VLSI Design (EVD)

Semiconductor integrated circuits (Gs) are the backbone of the current revolution in electronics
and computing. The Indian goverment has recently launched India Semiconductor Mission with
a vision to enable India's emergence as a global hub in electronics manufacturing and design.
Given ihe increasing interest of nation in semiconductor manufacturing and R&D, both in India
and worldwide, the sector is likely to witness a spike in demand for engineers with

semiconductor Knowhow. The objective of the new B.Tech, in Electronics and VLSI Design
(EVD) at DA-IICT is to create industry ready undergraduate manpower for tho semiconductor
industry and create a vibrant ecosystem.

The proposed B.Tech. in EVD degree is a cutting edge course, allowing the student io choose
from a wide range of modules based on digital integrated circult design and analogue integrated
circuit design, This is an interdisciplinary program, allowing students fo take various courses as
part of the program. There are two pathways offered to the students where he / she can take
various elective courses ranging from Circuits and Systems, Solid State Devices, VLSI!
Technology, Embedded Processors, Sensors and Systems, Real Time Operating Systems, loT,
Secure Devices, MEMS along with opportunity to take various discipline and free electives
leading to specialization either in Electronic System Design or VLSI Design.

Presentation on B.Tech. in Electronics and VLSI Design program was made by Prof Biswajit
Mishra. He presented the genesis, need, program structure and commitment and

requirement of / from the instidtute.

Key points of the new UG program B.Tech. in Electronics and VLSI Design:

1. First UG program dedicated to VLSI in Gujarat

2. Innovative and Industry relevant courses

3. Practice based curriculum

4. Student will be designing an Integrated Chip during their course duration in ll year.
5. An entrepreneurship route if the student decides to venture out

Observations made by AC members arc as follows

« The proposed curriculum is timely and innovative and will make students industry
ready

« There are several pathways which gives flexibility to the student to explore in
research, industry or entrepreneurship.

« The focused and practical and industry relevant aspects in the course is encouraging

are benelitied.

as an honours course or suggest a mechanism to bring all the UG programs to
have equal number of credit structure.

» Exploratory Project should be introduced

«in the next review meeting, the coordinator should report the good practices and
challenges and progress of (he program

« The two programming course offered to the students during the first yaar may be
looked at and ways to merge them inte one, if required.

« Research internships MoU shoukd be in place and to be treated as a priority,

« As the Math courses lay emphasis on the applications, these should be renamed to
Engineering Maths.

MINUTES OF THE 41st MEETING OF THE ACADEMIC COUNCIL HELD
ON 16 JULY 2025

The 41st meeting of the Academic Council was held on 16 July 2025.
The following members were present:

Professor Tathagata Bandyopadhyay, in Chair
Professor Sukumar Nand

Professor Madhavan Mukund

Professor Maniklal Das

Professor Bhaskar Chaudhury

Professor Anish Mathuria

Professor Madhumita Mazumdar

ao on eo

Leave of Absence:

1. Professor Prabir Biswas
2. Shri Vinod Mall

Non-Member Secretary: Mr. Siddharth Swaminarayan

The Chairman welcomed all the members. The Executive Registrar informed the
Council that Shri Pavitar Singh, our alumnus, has been appointed as a member of
the Board, In his place, Shri Vinod Mall, a senior and active alumnus, has been
appointed to the Council.

Leave of absence was granted to Shri Vinod Mall and Professor Prabir Biswas for
reasons noted by the Chairman.

Agenda Item No. 1

Confirmation of the Minutes and Action Taken Report of the 40th Meeting held
on 10 July 2025

The Executive Registrar briefed the Council on the action taken on the
recommendations of the previous meeting and informed that there were no changes
in the draft minutes circulated earlier. With a correction to include Professor
Madhumita Mazumdar’s name in the list of attendees, theGe
minutes of the 40th meeting.

Agenda Item No, 2

Noting of Government of Gujarat Notification Declaring the University as a
Centre of Excellence (CoE)

The Executive Registrar informed the Council that the Government of Gujarat has
conferred CoE status upon the top ten technical and professional institutions in
Gujarat. DA-IICT has been awarded this recognition for the second consecutive
term, now extended to six academic years (2025-26 to 2030-31), unlike the three-
year term in the first cycle.

One major privilege in this cycle is the increased intake under lhe management
quota in the BTech and MTech programs—trom 67% to 75%. Accordingly, the State
Government will allot only 25% of the intake through its centralized admission
process.

The Council took note of the development with appreciation for the collective efforts
led by the Deans and Director General.

Agenda Item No. 3
Recommendations of the Board of Studies for the MDes Programs

Professors Anirban Dutla Gupta and Anupam Rana made a detailed presentation on
two key recommendations:

i. A comprehensive review and revision of the MDes in Communication Design
curriculum, effective from AY 2025-26.

ii. Introduction of a néw specialization—MDes in Intelligent User Experience
Design (iUXD), effective from AY 2025-26, with a market-oriented and innovative
curriculum structure.

The Council appreciated the thorough planning, involvement of external experts, and

academic rigor in (he proposals and approved the recommendations of the Board of
Studies.

Agenda Item No. 4
Ratification and Approval of Academic Calendar 2025-26

The Council noted and approved the decision of the Director General to announce
the academic calendar for the Academic Year 2025-26. ;

Page 2 of 4

175

Agenda Item No. 5

Proposal for Revision in Policy on Course Repetition for the grade
improvement or backlog, and Introduction of Summer Courses for PG
Students

Professor Tapas Maiti presented the rationale behind the proposed policy changes.
The Council made the following observations:

i. Insert a clause in the grade improvement policy allowing the best of the two
attempts to be considered.

ii. Address inconsistency in the grade requirements for UG (5 CPI) and PG (6 CPI)
students for backlog/grade improvement. The current proposal includes CD grade in
PG eligibility—this requires further review to align UG and PG norms.

iii, Consider offering MOOC courses (Massive Open Online Courses) during the
summer for PG students—particularly useful since many will be on internships,
These may include core and elective courses, with or without a cap on the number of
COUrSes.

The Council authorized the Director General to further deliberate on these points and
finalize the revised policy.

Agenda Item No. 6
Faculty Recruited (January — June 2025)

The Council noted with appreciation the recruitment of nine new facully members
during this period, as per the report presented.

Tabled Agenda ltem

Agenda Item No. 7
Extension of Academic Timeline for PhD Scholar Pramod Tripathi

The Council ratified the Director General's decision—based on RPS Committee
recommendations—to grant an extension up to Autumn (December) 2025 to PhD
scholar Pramod Tripathi (201721003) to complete his academic requirements,
including the PhD synopsis exam, in view of personal hardships faced during (he

pandemic,

Agenda Item No. 8
Recommendations of the Board of Studies for PhD in Law — School of Law

Professor Nisheet Upadhyay presented the proposal, draft regulations, and
recommendations of the Board of Studies for the PhD in Law program.

The Council approved, in principle, the introduction of the PhD in Law program from
AY 2025-26, subject to incorporation of minor suggestions from the Council.

The draft regulations were subsequently sent to Professor (Dr) William Nunes,
Gujarat National Law University, Gandhinagar, for review and integration of Council
observations.

Annexure |: Approved PhD in Law Regulations, after required revisions.

The meeting concluded with a vote of thanks to the Chair.

Annexure XIV

Policy on Examination
System

ith!

Annexure XIV

Policy on Examination System at DAIICT

I. Introduction
This policy outlines the framework for the conduct of examinations at DAICT,
managed by the Controller of Examinations (Coli). [t applies to all students enrolled tn
various programs at the institute, ‘he cxamination system is designed to ensure
fairness. transparency, and integrity in evaluating student performance.
2. Examination Strocture DAICT follows a structured examination system that consists
of the following:
In-Semester Examinations: Where are typically two in-semester examinations
conducted during the course of the semester:
= First In-Semester Examination
» Second-Semester Examination
» End-Semester Examination: An cxam in pen-paper mode, as announced in the
Academic Calendar is condueted by the Cok of typically three hours of timestot.
+, Examination Calendar The dates for all exams are pre-scheduled and published in the
Academic Calendar before the start of cach semester,
4. Examination Process

a, Examination Timetable: (he Cok releases the timetable for cach exam at feast two
weeks prior to the examination date. The exam duration and weightage are predefined
by the course instructors,

b. 1D Cards: All students are required to carry their valid ID for entry into the
examination hall.

¢. Seating Arrangements: Seuting arrangements are posted outside the examination
venues. Students are required to adhere strictly to the seating plan and maintain silence
in the examination hall.

d. Use of Materials: Only authorized materials, as specified by the course instructor.
are allowed during the examination. The use of unauthorized materials (including but
not limited ta clectronic gadgets) is strictly prolubited,

&. Examination Rules and Conduct

a. Attendance: Students must be present at the examination venue al least 13 minutes
before the start lime.

b. Identity Verification: Students must display their valid student 11) upen entering the
examination venue. The invigilators perform checks during the exam,

ce. Code of Conduct: Students are expected to maintain discipline during the
examination, Cheating, talking. exchanging materials. or any other form of malpractice

|

6. Disciplinary Actions for Malpractice

a. Detection: Any student found engaging in unfair practices during examinations will
be reported to the Col: immediately.

bh. Consequences: Depending on the severity of the malpractice, action will be taken as
per nificd malpractice policies.

7. Responsibilities of Students
Familiarize themselves with the examination schedule and requirements,
Adhere to all examination rules and instructions provided by invigilators.

%. Responsibilities of the Controller of Examinations (Cok)
Te oversee the smooth conduct of all examinations.
‘To ensure timely announcement of results.
lo address grievances related to examinations in accordance with institute
policies,

The Colt office consists of the following personnel:

« Controfler of Examinations (Cok): Responsible for overseeing the entire
CMIMInaLiON process, ensuring adherence to policies, and addressing any cxamination-
related issues.

+ Joint Controller of Examinations (Jt. CoE): Assists the Col! in managing
examination activities, including handling day-to-day operations.

« Examination Assistant: Provides administrative support, including logistics.
communication with faculty, and cnsuring compliance with examination guidelines,

The Col office handles the following tasks:
i. Question Paper Management:

» Collection of question papers from faculty members,
* Ensuring confidentiality and sceurity in the printing and distribution of question
pulperrs.
b. Examination Timetable:
* Preparing and announcing the examination timetable at least two weeks before the
CORUM,

Annexure XV

Policy on Evaluation
Practices

Annexure XV

Policy on Evaluation Practices

Assessment is an integral part of the education system as it certifies the academic standards
accomplished by a student. Faimess and transparency in assessing students’ performance ensure
impartial judgment, in achieving students’ confidence and quality education in the programme.
In order to achieve the fairness and transparency in the evaluation system, the institute fits

adopted the following practices.

(i) The course instructor announces to students about the grading policy and the distribution
of weightage in different components in the courses that the instructor is wing to follow
to assess the students’ performance in the course, The performance of the students ts
assessed on a continuous basis by conducting two in-semester examinations, end-
semester examination, assignments, quizzes, student presentations, lab work, and
projects. A minimum of 80% attendance is required in each course for appearing for the
end semester examination, After evaluation, the answer books are shown to the students
so that they can see where they have made mistakes and discuss with the instructor
accordingly. The semester result is announced within a week after the end semester

examination,

(ii) All our academic programmes follow continuous evaluation methods in which weightage
is assigned to different components. namely, assignments, laboratory work. quizzes,
projects, ete. In addition to continuous assessment, the academic calendar allows
instructors to conduct two in-semester examinations and an end-semester examination for
courses offered in a semester. Each instructor is responsible for the evaluation process for
the course(s) taught by him/her. The instructor informs the students of the evaluation
process of the course at the beginning of the semester. The examination pattern is decided
by the instructor and communicated to students in the beginning of the semester. Typical
examination patterns include closed book, open book, objective, subjective, multipte
choice, etc. If student misses any exams due to ill health or family urgencies, then the

respective course instructor arranges make up exams/labs within two weeks afler the

missed exams schedule. Dean (AP) and conveners of UG and PG committees oversee the

(iin @ typical university system which runs many progarmmes under different departments,
Core courses may involve multiple batches across the departments or affiliated colleges.
Furthermore, the same course may be taught by many instructors in various departments
or affiliated colleges. In such scenarios, it is advisable to have the final examination
question paper moderated by external examiners. However, in our system, the Core
courses are run by a single instructor for a single batch. As a result, unfair means or
unsecure circumstances do not arise in our system. UG/PG committees along with Dean
(AP) keep a continuous watch to the regular lectures/lwhs, course outlines, feedback
processes and examination & evaluation practices. For any issues, they discuss the matter

with the instructor concerned to achieve the quality academic standards.

Cv)AN our courses with laborttory components are being assisted hy Teaching Assistants
(MTech and PhD students), The lab component of the course is mentored hy the course
Instricor with support of multiple teaching assistants. A student performance is his/her
lab components are assessed continually by both teaching assistants and the instructor, In
addition to the continuous assessment in lab sessions, the lab course is also having lab

Ex OTIS, vi Wa-VOce, eb,

(iv) The curricula of our programmes consist of many Core and Foundational courses which
are specialized in the ICT disciplines. It is not an easy task to identify extemal experts for
such curses in neighbouring institutions. Therefore, it was decided by the academic
uiministration of the institute that peer review and discussion would be a substitute for
Ihe course delivery, which ts being practiced will date. However, new instituiions like ITs
and 1Ts have recently been established in the neighbouring regions. so in mear terms the
institute would like to involve external expertsfor the question paper moderation and UG

and PCr thesis progress evaluation.

189

Annexure XVI

Policy on Question Paper
Setters, Board of
Examinations and

Invigilators

Annexure AVI

All our programmes follow semester system, Dean( AP) assigns courses for every programme lo
instructors after discussion with various peer groups of the institute. The instructor decides the
weightages of the in-semester and end-semester examinations, quizzes, assignments, laboratory
and project work for the course he/she is assigned for. The distnbution of weightages ts
announced to students by the course instructor in the beginning of the course, so that the students
are aware of the evaluation mechanism to be followed in the course, The course instructor
follows one of the following grading mechanisms, after announcing the same in the beginning of
the semester — (i) the absolute grading is based on a predetermined class intervals allotted for
each grade. Stodent marks are converted to grades based on the predetermined class intervals
announced for the course;(ii) the relative grading is based on the distribution of marks obtained
by all the students of the course and the grades are awarded based on a cut-off marks or
percentile.

The course instructor sets the question paper for in-semester and end-semester examinations. The
question paper of a course must consider the course grading policy, weightage assigned to
different components of the course, which were being announced to students by the course
instructor in the beginning of the semester. The instructor doesthe evaluation of the answer sheets
for his‘her courses. Students are given the opportunity to inspect the marks awarded for all
components of the evaluation process and to see their examinations answer-books prior to the
declaration of results. The evaluation process of each course is completely transparent to the
students that should reflect what the instructor had announced in the beginning of the semester

about the course grading policy and what has been followed for the final grading.

Internships and UG Projects Evaluation Board

Separate coordinator is appointed by Dean (AP) for Intemship!Project, who supervises the entire
task with the support of faculty and staff,

Intermships and projects provide an opportunity to the student to carry out projects either in-

191

campus or off-campus. An in-cimpus intemship/project should have a faculty mentor supervisor
a DA-NCT, whe would look after the project work of the student and interact closely with the
student, Whereas, the off-campus internship’ praject requires on-site supervisor as well o faculty

mentor from DA-TCT. who would interact with the on-site supervisor as and when required.

Student is required to defend! present his/her internship’project work somewhere in the second
week of March for the Rural Intermship, in the second week of July for the Summer
Research‘Industrial internship, and in the first week of May for the BTech project. Multiple
panels of faculty evaluate each student's performance on the basis of originality of the work,
technical contents, and outcomes of the work that the student has carried out during the

intemship! project.

AlSe¢ Projects Evaluation

MSc projects are examined by a panel of faculty constituted by the programme coordinator in
consultation with PG convener and Dean(AP). Almost all students do their final project off-
cumpus. Their projects are being supervised by the on-site project supervisor, who forwards
hisher recommended seore for the student's performance to the MSe project covndinator. All
sludents are asked to make their presentation in front of a panel of faculty at the end of the
semester. The panel sees the score suggested by the on-site supervisor and based on the student's
perfonmance showed in his‘her presentation, the final grade is recommended to the project

coordinator, who then upboads the grade in E-campus.

MTech Theses Evaluation

The programme coordinator in consulation with PG convener and Dean{AP) constitutes the
examination panel for MTech thesis. Each MTech thesis is mentored by a three member panel
comprising the superviser(s) and subject experts. Each thesis goes through first stage, second
stage and final stage evaluation by the same panel. Once the panel clears the final stage with
sulistuctory assessment. the student is allowed to submit the final thesis. which will be examined
an internal expert. Upon satisfactory evaluation report by the thesis examiner, the student can
defend his‘her thesis publicly in the presence of the thesis evaluation panel and thesis examiner,
Nevertheless, the institute can invite extemal examiners for theses evaluation as and when the

demand arises.

1

PhD Theses Evaluation

The PhD evaluation process is similar to other premier institutions. Once a PhD student
completes the stipulated course work and clears the comprehensive examination, the Dean( AP)
constitute a Research Progress Committee (RPC) comprising with three faculty member
including the PhD supervisor for evaluating the student's progress in every semester. The RPC
evaluates the progress in every semester made by the student and provides feedback/suggestion
accordingly, Before the thesis submission, a pre-synopsis presentation is evaluated by a separate
committee to judge the quality and the quantity of the work that has been carried out by the
student. After getting positive feedback from the pre-synopsis committee, the student is
permitted to submit his‘her thesis.

PhD theses are examined by the external examiners followed by viva voce examination. The
examiners dre appointed by the Director and at least one of the examiners is selected from
overseas. Once the thesis examiners accept the thesis, the Dean (AP) asks the student to defend
his/her thesis. In the PHO thesis defense, at beast one external examiner attends the presentation
made by the students. The institute also invites the overseas examiner to attend the presentation

on skype.

Grievance Redressal

The institute has a formal mechanism in place for addressing all grievances pertaining to
academics, admission and student life. The institute has constituted Grievance Redressal
Committee (GRC) per UGC guidelines, which comprises Dean (AP) as the chairman, Dean
(Students), UG and PG committee conveners as members, and Registrar as convener of the

GRC.

To address student grievances for course delivery, examination and evaluation, the following
mechanism is cared out at various levels at the institute:
® The institute has an clected Academic Commitice of SBG (Student Body Government),
which acis as an interface between the student community and the faculty for all
acudemic matiers. All the suggestions regarding academic issues by students are brought

to this Committee, which, after discussion and consultation, passes on the suggestions to

the UG andor PG Committee. After the discussions in this committee the matter is
passed onto Deon-AP for appropriate actions.

® The Academic Committee of SBG is mentored by the Dean-AP. The academic calendar
staling the schedule of all examinations (in-semester and end-senvester) is communicated
to all students by the Registrar's office well in advance before commencement of the
academic seston. If any complaints noticed by the Academic Committee of SBG or
students), then they bring the matter to the notice of UG/PG committees and to Deun-AP.
Dean-AP in consultation with UG and PG committees addresses the matter appropriately
und forwards the recommendation (if any) to the Regisirar’s office. The Registrar's
office, then, can amend the examinations schedule appropriately and can submit the sume
forapproval ofthe Acudemic Council of the institute,

» For eich course, the instructor announces the grading policy in ihe very first lecture: of
the course. The grading policy details about the vanous components of the course and the
weightage attached to cach component (c.g. in-semester exam, end-semester exam,
WuiZves, Assinments, presentations). If a student has any complaints on quizes of
assignments then he/she talks to the respective instructor and resolves the issues, Lf the
complaint is related to in-semester or end-semester examinations then the matter is
referred 10 Dean-AP, who then in consultation with UG/PG convener instructs the course
instructor to take needful action (ce. make-up test. re-exam, re-checkine) an the matter.

® For any complaints related to marks or @rade. the mutter is referred io the course
instructor, The instrector shows the answer sheet and clarifies the poinis wherever the
siudent has complaints, If the matter is eligible for marks/grade improvement then the
instructor submits a hardcopy fonm in the preseribed form for grade improvement with
justification for improvement to Gean-AP, Then, Dean-AP checks its correctness and
forwards the form lo Registrar's office and the modified grade is then reflected in the
student's grade sheet accordingly, If the grievances are unresolved, then Dean-AP
consulis with the Director and refers the complaints to an expert (internal/external)
Mi judicator.

With the aforementioned efforts, the institute is able to resolve all grievances penaining to

course objectives, examination and ¢cvaluation effectively.

Annexure XX

Minutes of Finance
Committee Meeting for
proposed Fees

708

Annexure XX

DHIRUBHAI AMBANI UNIVERSITY, GANDHINAGAR

MINUTES OF THE TWENTY-THIRD MEETING OF THE FINANCE COMMITTEE

The 23™ meeting of the Finance Committee was held on 30" January 2025 in a hybrid
mode in the Board Room of the Institute.

The following were present:

1) Professor Tathagata Bandyopadhyay, Director General, in Chair
2) Shr Shrikant Kulkarni, Member

3) Shn Umesh Dalal, Member

4) Professor Maniklal Das

Shri Shrikant Kulkarni and Shri Umesh Dalal, Members, participated in the meeting,
through the video conference mode.

The following items were discussed:

ltemn No, 1

To propose revisions to our existing fee structure for all the academic
programmes and the hostel fees for the students to be admitted in academic
year 2025-26

Background Note:

The Education Department, Government of Gujarat, vide its notification dated 4th
January 2022 has declared seven private universities in the State of Gujarat as Centre
of Excellence (COE) for three years. Ve are one of the seven
universities. Subsequently, by a notification dated 7th March 2023, the COEs are
exempted from certain provisions of the Gujarat Professional Technical Educational
Colleges or Institutions (Regulation of Admissions and Fixation of Fees) Act 2007. As
one of the conditions of the said notification, COEs are required to notify/declare the
fee structure for the professional programs (BE-BTech, ME-MTech for us) to the
Appellate Committee, the proposed fee structure for the next academic year. As per
one of the provisions, if the AC within three weeks of receipt of the declaration finds
that the proposed fee structure substantially constitutes profiteering, the declaration
shall be referred back with comments to enable such institution to modify the fee
structure accordingly and shall not implement the proposed fee structure.

Accordingly, for the last AY 2024-25, after the due internal process, declared and
informed the Appellate Committee (AC) about our revised fees for the BTech and

programs (copy attached). A statement showing the fee structure (increase in
comparison to the previous year) of our all academic programs and hostel fees for the
AY 2024-25 is also attached for reference.

A comparative statement of fees for the AY 2024-25 by the five COEs for their BTech
Program, including DAIICT, is attached herewith for your kind perusal. In the said
statement, from the current websites of Ahmedabad University and PDEU, we have
also mentioned the proposed fee for their Blech Program, published for the next AY
2025-26 admissions.

Matter for consideration:

The Finance Committee, as per the directives given by the Board of Governors in its
18th January 2025 meeting, is requested to consider and propose revisions to our
existing fee structure for all the programs for the students to be admitted in AY 2025-
26, and also for the existing batches of the students.

Resolution:

The Committee was appraised with the relevant Notification of Education Department,
Government of Gujarat and prescribed process described in it for the Centre of
Excellence institutions for the professional programmes offered by the Institutes. The
Committee was also appraised with the following documents.

a) Our last academic years fees structure proposed to the Appellate Committee
vide our letter dated 9" February 2024.

b) Supporting documents to be attached with our proposal, for example, CA
certified stalaments (of the previous year) of income from the tuition fee and
expenditures of permissible expenses, as per the SOPs prescribed by the State
Government, along with all related schedules and annexures towards imparting
education in the BTech and MTech Programs and a consolidated statement for
all other Programs, to work out per student actual expenditures incurred and
per student income received by the institute.

c) Appellate Committee's Order dated 14" August 2024 approving our proposal
to increase the tuition fees in the professional programmes (BTech and MTech).

d) Comparative statement of tuition fees charged by prominent COEs during the
last AY and the fees proposed by some of them for the next AY 2025-26.

After due deliberation and examining the above documents, the Committee
recommended the following fees for all our programs, per semester, for the students
to be admitted in academic year 2025-26.

For the Tech Program proposed tuition fee, it was recommended to increase the
teaching assistantship (stipend) from the existing Rs. 12,500/ to Rs. 15,000/ per
month. By this, the proposed annual tuition fees paid by the MTech students, i.e.
Rs.1,60,000/ will be fully repaid by the institute to the MTech students by way of
teaching assistantship stipend amounts to them.

As the meeting was called for the above matter only, there were no further agenda
item/s, and the meeting ended with a vote of thanks to the Chair.

Faculty Recruitment Committee and Faculty Recruitment Process

The Faculty Recruitment Committee (FRC) is constituted area-wise that comprises the Dean
(Faculty), the Dean (AP). the area coordinator, a member of the Faculty Development and
valuation Committee (FOEC). and an area member other than the coordinator.

The role of Arca-wise FRC ts to screening candidates based on their eligibility criteria and
requirenvent of the respeelive Areas, and shortlisting a filtered set of candidates for the
interview by the Seleetion panel comprising External experts, Director, Dean( Faculty),
Dean( Academic Programs) and internal FRC members,
