Faculty Recruitment recess

The faculty recruitment process has a major role to play in shaping the future of the institute.
which ts faculty driven and aligned with the requirement of the institute. The input from the
faculty members iseextremely important. and so is its quality. In this prowess the Arca members
are considered to be the domain experts whe would evaluate the applicant's potential as a
teacher (usually based on histher teaching seminar), a researcher (usually based on the
publications. research seminar and personal interaction) and finally as a person (usually based
on personal interaction) keeping the area's requirement in view. The evaluations should be
explicated with reasons and. most imporianily should not to be influenced by others so that the
FRC reeeives diverse perspectives that enhances the chance of making a correct decision. The
collated feedback from the members should be kept confidential. and should be shared only
with the FRC members.

Stages of the FRO process:

Stage 1: On receipt of the application the Dean( Faculty) will pre-screen candidates based on
the candidates’ eligibility critena and academic credentials, and then forward a filtered set of
candidates to the Arca Coordinator (AC) for initiating the evaluation process, If the AC
receives an application, the coordinator will forward it to the Dean (Faculty) to keep the Dean
(Faculty) in the loop. (fa faculty member reecives an application, she/he will forward ito the
Dean (Faculty) and the AC.

Stage 2: The AC will forward the application to the area members requesting their feedback.
The quality of journal/conference publications, research statenvent and teaching statement are
indicative parameters for assessing a candidate. The Arca members will sai their written
feedback to the AC. The AC will then collate the feedbacks and He PURAERC prior to the

meeting. The FRC members from the area don’t need to send their feedback prior to the
meeting.

Stage 3: The AC will convene a meeting of ihe FRC, Based on the area feedback as well as
their own judgment the FRC would decide whether to call the candidate for a teaching
presentation which can be conducted Online. The candidate's presentation and interaction with
some internal domain experts in the Arca will help the AC in consultation with the Dean
(Faculty) to shortlist a candidate for the final round of the selection prowess. The AC will factor
fecdback received from the intemal domain experts while deciding whether to call a candidate
for campus wisit or not,

Stage 4; Based on the outcomes of the Stage 3, Dean (Faculty) will inform the candidates. 1
the decision is negative, the Dean (Faculty) will send a regret letter. Ifthe decision is positive,
then the AC in consultation with the Dean (Faculty) will fix the dates for the candidate's
campus visit (a two-day wisit) aller checking the availability of the members of the PRC, the
Arca members, the Director and the cundidate.

Stape §: The Dean (Faculty) in consultation with the Director will invite the extemal experts
to be called for conducting interviews and would send a formal invitation to the candidate for
campus visit. The schedule of candidates’ visit to the campus will be decided by the AC in
consultations with Dean{Faculty), The candidate will be given domestic travel support,
economy class air fare by shortest route, for attending the interview and local hospitality during
his/her stay. Candidate from abroad may be permitted to attend the interview through virtual
mode or when visiting India in a mutually convenient schedule.

Stage 6: Candidates will be invited to visit the campus for wo days — on the first day they will
interact with some Area members and on the second day they will be interviewed by a selection
panel consisting of internal and external domain experts. During the interaction with the
candidate ihe Area members are supposed to pet a sense of the suitability of the candidate as a
colleague both intellectually and as a person, ‘The research and teaching seminar, and the
candidate's interview shall be scheduled on the next day. The AC will collate fecdhack of the
Arca members interacted with the candidate and feedback of faculty members attended in the
cundidate’s research presentation, then send the collated feedback to the Dean (Faculty }on the
same day, before the candidate appears for his/her interview with the external experts. External
experts are typically invited from ITs, 1Se and similar premier institutions.

Stave 7: The candidate's interview by the external experts will be attended by the Director.
Dean(Faculty), Dean (Academic Programs) and one internal domain expert in the Area, Dcan
(Faculty) will share with the experts about the feedback received from the AC for the candidate.
Based on the candidate's performance the experts panel will take a final decision whether an
offer can be made or neat.

Timeline

Stages | and 2 isa batch process once ina month, In case of an exceptionally good upplicant, the
process needs to be expedited.
Stages 3 and 4 usually ina month, However, it may sometimes pet delayed bepage

Overall, a candidate whether selected or nod will be decided in a 3-month timeline from his/her
applicaiicn dive,

applica, feedbacks of the Area members. minutes of the FRC mectings should be preserved,

Faculty Self-Appraisal and Evaluation Process

Sclf-Appraisal of faculty is collected during March-April of each year and assessed by the Dean
(Vaculty) and the Director, Faculty performance is assessed based on his/her teaching performance.
research outcomes and contributions to ad ministrative services.

A, Contributions to Teaching

A faculty member is required to teach three courses in an academic year. The Self-Appraisal form
captures the course details (core, elective) and student feedback, labs development of individual
faculty. Typically, a score above 4 (out of 5) is considered as Good, between 3.5 and 4 is considered
as Satisfactory, and below 3.5 is Not Satishactory.

3. Contributions to Research and Consultancy

Research contribution is measured through multiple metrics, namely, publications, books, patents,

projects, consultancy. The following criteria is used to assess the research outcomes ofa faculty.

Annexure XXVII

Board of Governors (BoG)
minutes of meeting

Annexure XXVIII

DHIRUBHAI AMBANI INSTITUTE OF INFORMATION
AND COMMUNICATION TECHNOLOGY
GANDHINAGAR

MINUTES OF THE TWENTY-FIRST MEETING OF THE
BOARD OF GOVERNORS

The Twenty-First meeting of the Board of Governors was held on 27" January
2024 at 11.00 am at DA-IICT Gandhinagar.

The following members were presen.

Ambassador T.S. Tirumurti, in Chair

Dr Alok Nath De

. Shri Shrenik Vaishnav

. Shri Shrikant Kulkarni

Ms. Alona J Doshi, through video conference

Shri Punit Garg, through video conference

Professor Tathagata Bandyopadhyay, Director

Professor Maniklal Das, Dean- Faculty

Professor Bhaskar Chaudhury, Dean- Academic Programmes

oon bw +

=

Leave of absence:

Mis. Tina Ambani

. Shri Anmol Ambani

. Professor Tridip Subrud

. Professor Bimal Kumar Roy

_ Shri Mukesh Kumar, |AS, Principal Secretary (Higher and Technical Education)
Education Department, Govt of Gujarat

Ms. Mona Khandhar, IAS, Principal Secretary (l/C), Department of Science and
Technology, Govt of Gujarat

in & i Po

om

Non-Member Secretary: Shri Siddharth Swaminarayan, Executive Registrar

At the outset, it was informed to the members that the President, Ms. Tina Ambani,
has nominated Shri T.S. Tirumurti to Chair meeting and to Preside the Convocation
Ceremony to be held in the evening.

The members were briefed about the detailed schedule of the day,

The Chairman welcomed all the members and adopted th a items of the
meeting.

The Board noted and accepted the request of Professor Abhay Karandikar to accept
his résignation from the Board on becoming the Seeretary of the Department of
Science and Technology, Government of India.

The Board noted with thanks his contributions as a member since July 2021.
Agenda item 1
To confirm the minutes of the Board meeting held on January 28, 2023.

The Board was informed that no remarks/observations were received from the
members on the minutes of the above-referred meeting. The members confirmed the
minuies,

Agenda Item 2
Action taken report on the recommendations of the Board in its 20 meeting.

The Board noted the actions taken on the minutes of the meeting held on 28% Ja nuary
2023

Concerning the decision taken in the last meeting on the tagline “Engineers with Social
Responsibilities,” the Director briefed the Board that the faculty, in general, are
uncomfortable with the word “Engineers” given that the institute offers programs in
humanities, data science, agricultural analytics, science, and design. It may be
broadened to subsume all these disciplines. The Board discussed the matter and
recommended developing a few taglines involving faculty members that align with the
institute's vision and mission. The taglines may be pul up before the Board (by sending
@ proposal by circulations) for further consideration,

The Board also suggested registering our logo under the Trade Marks Act in two
separate forms.

i) Only logo
ii) Logo with the tagline

Agenda Item 3

To consider the recommendations of the Academic Council to confer the
Degrees and the President's Gold Medals to the eligible students in the 18"
Convocation to be held on 27" January 2024.

The Board perused the recommendations of the Academic Council in its meeting held
on 19" January 2024, accepted them, and adopted the following resolutions:

“Resolved that the 65 students of Bachelor of Technology (Honours) in Information
and Communication Technology with a Minor in Computational Science: 301 students
of Bachelor of Technology in Information and Communication Technology: 10 students
of Doctor of Philosophy; 13 students of Master in Design (Communication Design); 44
studenis of Master of Science (Data Science); 121 students of Master of Science

Systems and Machine Leaming): 1 student of Master of Technology (Electronics and
Communication); and 51 students of Master of Technology (Information and
Communication Technology) who have completed their graduation requirements and
whose names appearing in the list placed before the meeting, to be conferred
respective Degrees at ihe Convocation to be held on 27" January 2024"

“Also resolved to adopt the recommendations of the Academic Council to award the
President's Gold Medal to the following students in the seven Programmes.”

201901162 | RATHOD SIDDHARTH VANRAJSINH B Tech (ICT)

B Tech (Honours)
201901028 | KEDAR BHAVYA HASMUKHLAL 9.2 ICT with minor CS
VINAY SHETH M Tech (ICT)
202115008 | SAI SAI ADITYA PUSULURI M Tech (CS&ML)
202112083 | VIVEK JAYESH DOSHI
RAVAL RUSHI DEVENDRABHAI Bae M Des (CD)

The members also suggested to revisit awarding of Gold Medals keeping in view the
size of the cohort of students in different programs.

Agenda Item 4
To ratify the decision of the Academic Council to launch a new Programme
6 Tech In Electronics and VLSI Design from AY 2023-24

The Board ratified the Academic Council's decision to launch the new GB. Tech
Programme in Electronics and VLSI Design from academic year 2023-24.

Following were ihe observations and recommendations of the Board on the
presentation made by Professor Biswajit Mishra, Programme Coordinator, on the
current status of the Programme after tts launch.

i, The Programme Coordinator was requested to assess the infrastructure
required for the labs to be set up, considering the number of students
admitted and the lead time needed to sei up the Jabs. The Institute should
explore possible collaborations with the relevant industries and check the
possibilities of Government funding schemes for the_VLSI Design/
Semiconductor infrastructure. eo =

ii, Explore the possibilities for MoU with foreign universities collaborating with
leading semiconductor industries like Foxconn, Micron, etc.

lil. Also, explore collaboration possibilities with the Indian Institute of Sciences,
Bengaluru, Department of Semiconductor Technology.

Agenda Item 5
Approval of the Institute's Annual Report for the academic year 2022-23

The Annual Report forthe AY year 2022-23 was circulated with the agenda and notes
of the meeting, and the same was placed on the table for the perusal of the Board.
The Board approved the Annual Report for the year 2022-23.

The Board suggested incorporating the Annual Accounts of the Institute in the Annual
Report from the next Annual Report.

Agenda Item 6

Ratification of the decision taken by the Chairman Finance Committee to
approve Audited Annual Accounts for the FY 2022-23.

The Board ratified the Audited Annual Accounts for the F'¥ 2022-23 approved by the
Chairman of the Finance Committee.

Agenda Item 7

Ratification of the decision of the Chairman of the Finance Committee for the
authorized signatories for operations of the bank accounts.

The Board ratified the above decision as an ongoing arrangement.

The Board suggested that the Finance Commiltlee reconsider the existing
arrangements and work out specific financial limitation conditions for the signatories,
preferably with two sets of authorized signatories.

Agenda Item 8

Proposal to revise Semester-wise tuition fees for all our Programmes and Hostel
Fees, etc.

The Board perused and deliberated on the recommendations of the duly appointed
Committee on the above matter. The Director briefed the Board that our fee structure
is relatively lower than some of the Centre of Excellence Universities in the State of
Gujaral, and the institute generates its revenue primarily from tuition fees. Moreover,
to recruit and retain quality faculty members, it is essential to pay the compensation at
least at par with the 7" Central Pay Commission, Besides, the institute needs to create
incentives for doing good research. =

The Board approved the recommendations of the Committee to revise the
Programme-wise Semester Tuition Fees and Hostel Fees as per the following details,
w.e.f, Academic year 2024-25. The revised fee structure shall apply to the students
admitted from the AY 2024-25. Considering inflationary factors, the Institute will
increase the following fees by 7-10 percent annually for the batches of students to be
admitted from the AY 2024-25 and alter that.

The Board also recommended enhancing the amount spent on financial

Agenda Item No, 2

To confirm the minutes of the Board of Governors meeting held on January 27,
2024.

The Board was informed that no remarks/observaltions were received from the
members on the minutes of the above-referred meeting, circulated earlier.

The members confirmed the minutes

Agenda Item No. 3: To note the enactment of the Dhirubhai Ambani Institute of
Information and Communication Technology (Amendment) Act, 2024 [DA-IICT
(Amendment) Act, 2024]

Members perused the detailed notes attached with the agenda, especially, the salient
features, principal changes like change of the name of the university from Ohirubhai
Ambani Institute of Information and Communication Technology to Dhirubhai Ambani
University and enabling the university to transform into a Multidisciplinary Education
and Research University (MERU) as envisaged under the National Education Policy
(NEP), 2020,

Ms. Mona Khandhar, Principal Secretary, Department of Science and Technology,
Government of Gujarat suggested to introduce technology focussed academic
programmes in Semiconductor/VLS! Design, Quantum Computing, Data Security,
Cyber Security, etc., which are thrust areas identified by the Government of
India/Gujarat. As a Centre of Excellence (CoE), the University should pursue
collaborative educational and training programmes with other educational institutes
and relevant industries (on a hub and spoke model).

Professor Bandyopadhyay responded and highlighted that recognizing the need for
talent in the upcoming semiconductor industry, especially in the State of Gujaral, the
institute has already introduced a B.Tech. program in Electronics and VLSI Design,
which is now in its second year. He further added that the institute will work on her
suggestions.

Professor Tridip Suhrud suggested to design innovative training programs, spread
over & to 10 weekends, by the School of Law for working professionals, practicing
lawyers on contemporary issues such as newly enacted legal framework for criminal
laws ie. Bhartiya Nyaya Sanhita (BNS), Bhartiya Nagrik Surakhsha Sanhila (BNSS)
and Bhartiya Sakshya Adhiniyam (BSA). He further suggested that the School of Law
can publish impact analysis of various judgments / legal developments on a weekly
basis. Professor Dadhich, Director of Schoo! of Law will be requested to have a one-
on-one meeting with Professor Suhrud to have a detailed discussion in this matter.

Professor Suhrud also suggested to strengthen DA-IICT’s design education programs
by introducing information technology based innovative design programs involving
creative and practicing professionals in designing and delivering such programmes.
He further added that DAU should consider establishing a School of Design by offering
first-grade design programs for a focused batch of 60 students. Professor
Bandyopadhyay noted the suggestions and bnefed the Board that DA-IICT is
thoroughly reviewing its existing M. Des. Programme to make it more relevant and to
altract more aspirants, He also informed that DA-IICT is working on shaping a new M.
Des. Programme in Intelligent Experience Design and is planning to announce two
weekend short term courses on (i) Humanizing Technology with Phygital Products:
and (ii) Intelligent UX; Designing with the help of Al.

Ms. Alpna Doshi suggested to explore possibilities of designing courses in Artificial
Intelligence in Law and Intemational Taxation Laws. She also highlighted need for
exploring collaboration with global universities and actively seeking opportunities for
faculty and student exchange.

The Board placed on record its appreciation and gratitude for valuable support and
guidance extended by the senior leadership and government officers in the Higher &
Technical Education Department and other departments of the Government of
Gujarat, in completing a complex legislative process of DA-IICT Act Amendment, ina
timely and effective manner.

With the approval of the President, discussion on the Agenda ltem-4, mentioned
below, was taken up ahead of the Agenda ltem-3, as some of the members, who were
attending the meeting ina virtual mode, were in transit at the airport or in a road travel
and had requested for taking up the Agenda ltem-4 prior to the Agenda Item-3.

Agenda Item No. 4

To appoint Director General pursuant to the provisions of the DA-IICT
(Amendment) Act, 2024.

(During the discussion on this Agenda item, Professor (Dr.) Bandyopadhyay left the
Board Room)

The Board was apprised about amendments to the DA-IICT Act, 2003 which were
effectuated vide DA-IICT (Amendment) Act, 2024. It was highlighted that consequent
upon amendment to DA-IICT Act, 2003, Section § of the DA-IICT Act, 2003 stands
amended in keeping with the emerging multidisciplinary nature of the university. Vide
this amendment a university level position of ‘Director General’ has been created and
the amendment also recognized the fact that university will have multiple Directors
each leading a Higher Education Institution / a School core rehire

rt

It was further highlighted that the Dhirubhai Ambani University (DAU) will be taking
steps towards becoming a multidisciplinary university over next few years by adcing
different disciplines such as law, management, etc. This will entail shaping a strategy
and plans for the new schools to be added; building an integrated infrastructure al the
DAU Campus and offering interdisciplinary courses.

The Board was then briefed about the transformation agenda being pursued and
progress made in that regard by Prof. (Dr.) Tathagata Bandyopadhyay as the Director
of DA-IICT, since his appointment in November 2023, pursuant to the provisions of
DA-ICT Act, 2003.

Given that over the next 2 years or so, predominantly DAU will have DA-IICT (i.e.
School ef Technology) as the main operational institution and given Prof (Dr.)
Bandyopadhyay's long & rich experience and transformational agenda pursued by
him, it was proposed that Prof. (Dr.) Tathagata Bandyopadhyay be entrusted with the
responsibility of ‘Director General’ of DAU. Further it was proposed that in addition to
the role of Director General, he will continue to hold his current responsibility of
Director of DA-IICT.

The Board unanimously seconded the appointment of Prof (Or) Tathagata
Bandyopadhyay as the Director General of Dhirubhai Ambani University and resolved
as under:

RESOLVED THAT pursuant to the provisions of the DA-IICT (Amendment) Act, 2024.
which has amended the name of the institution from Dhirubhai Ambani institute of
information and Communication Technology (DA-IICT) to Dhirubhai Ambani
University (DAU), and introduced the position of ‘Director General’ at the university
level under Section 9 of the DA-IICT Act, 2003, the Board of Governors hereby accords
its approval for the appointment of Professor (Dr.) Tathagata Bandyopadhyay as the
Director General of Dhirubhai Ambani University.

RESOLVED FURTHER THAT this appointment shall be in accordance with Section
36 of the DA-IICT (Amendment) Act, 2024, which amended Section 34 of the DA-IICT
Act, 2003, enabling the Director to exercise the powers and perform the duties of the
Director General until such position is formally occupied.

RESOLVED FURTHER THAT in addition to the role of Director General of Dhirubhail
Ambani University, Prof. (Dr.) Tathagata Bandyopadhyay will continue ta hold his
current responsibility of Director of DA-IICT,

RESOLVED FURTHER THAT any necessary communication or statutory filings to
effectuate above resolutions be duly undertaken by any member of the Board of
Governors and/or the Executive Registrar and/or any person authorized by the
Executive Registrar in this regard. —<—=

(Amendment) Act, 2024 provisians.

The Board noted the proposed composition of the Board of Govemors of Dhirubhai

provisions, It was noted that Prof (Dr.) Tathagara Bandyopadhyay will be joining the

India. The Board also noted the profile of Shri Nikhil (Kunal) Dalal, which was placed

Agenda Item No. 8: To approve establishment of the School of Law pursuant to
the provisions of the DA-IICT (Amendment) Act, 2024 and appointment of
Director of School of Law, and introduction of executive education programs
and academic programs as envisaged under the plan for the School of Law.

The Board noted that the DA-IICT (Amendment) Act, 2024 has amended the Section
4 of the DA-IICT Act, 2003, in keeping with the emerging mulltidisciplinary nature of
the university. Section 4 of the DA-IICT Act, 2003 deals with the Objects of the
University. It was noted that the amended Section 4 now provides an exhaustive list
af disciplines in which Dhirubhai Ambani University (DAU) can provide knowledge,
education, training and research facilities of high order. It also recognizes that courses
offered can be of interdisciplinary nature combining various disciplines.

It was noted that as part of its journey to become a multidisciplinary university, over
the past one-year, considerable efforts have been undertaken and several
consultations held to develop an understanding of the landscape of legal education in
India and opportunities & challenges for a new law school to be established. This has
helped in shaping a strategy which aims at building a distinctive positioning for the
proposed School of Law under the aegis Dhirubhai Ambani Universily

The Board noted thal by following a robust selection process, a Founding Director has
been selected for establishing a School of Law. The selection process entailed
extensive interaction of the shortlisted candidates with Prof J. Ramachandran, who is
our Advisor and highly renowned Professor of Strategy, retired from |IM-Bangalore;
Prof. ‘Srividhya Ragavan, Bios tangle Law at Texas AGM oi School of Law,

Following the above-mentioned process, the Board noted that Prof. (Dr.) Avinash
Dadhich has been appointed as the Founding Director in July 2024 to lead the
establishment of the School of Law. Detailed profile of Prof. Dadhich was shared with
the members.

The Board was also briefed on ihe development path for the School of Law, as follows:

1, focusing on oulreach and executive education programs for legal and non-legal
professionals with immediate effect:

2. starting with Masters and PhD offering for a smaller batch by August 2025; and

3. launching offerings at the undergraduate level by August 2026.

The approval from Bar Council of India (BCI) is required for offering undergraduate
programs, The same shall be obtained before commencing the undergraduate
program(s) leading to LLB.

Progressively, over the years, OAU will consider adding other disciplines to its portfolio
vit. Management, Design, Architecture, etc.

After deliberations, the Board resolved as under:

RESOLVED THAT pursuant to the provisions of Section 4 of the DA-IICT Act, 2003,
as amended by the DA-IICT (Amendment) Act, 2024, which expands the University's
Objects to include multidisciplinary education and interdisciplinary courses, the
establishment of the School of Law at Dhirubhai Ambani University (DAU) he
and is hereby approved,

RESOLVED FURTHER THAT the introduction of the following programs and
initiatives as outlined in the strategy document is hereby approved, subject to fulfilment
of the University’s governance processes and necessary statutory and regulatory
approvals, as applicable:

1. Outreach and Executive Education Programs: For legal and non-legal
professionals to commence immediately.

*. Postgraduate and Doctoral Programs: To begin by August 2025.

3. Undergraduate Programs: To be launched by August 2026, subject to obtaining
requisite approval from the Bar Council of India (BCI).

RESOLVED FURTHER THAT the Board acknowledges the appointment of Prof. (Dr.}
Avinash Dadhich as the Founding Director of the School of Law.

RESOLVED FURTHER THAT the phased development approach for the Schoo! of
Law, which includes extensive stakeholder consultations and professional
engagements, is hereby noted and endorsed,

RESOLVED FURTHER THAT any necessary actions, communications, or statutory
filings required to implement above resolutions, including engagement with the Bar

frams,.be undertaken by
ny, the Founding

and authorized personnel,

To confirm the minutes of the Board of Governors meeting held on

The members confirmed the minutes, and noted the following updates on

I) The members were appraised of the concepts behind the new
logo and the process followed in selection of the visual identity of
the Dhirubhai Ambani University. Members found the identity
design inspirational, vibrant and conveying the rignt message

The members were also appraised of the fact that the design of

Agenda Item No. 3:
Director's Presentation

Professor Bandyopadhyay presented the transformational road map
planned, implemented and to be implemented, highlighting the
challenges, and initiatives taken on various institutional matters. Members
noted and appreciated it. Following are some of the observations and
suggestions by the board members.

I) Retention of faculty and attracting competent faculty members
should be given a prime attention.

ii) Use of new and emerging technologies in teaching is to be
encouraged.

ili) Processes are to be created for more alumni involvement in the
academic activities of the institute. Given that many of our alumni
are successful entrepreneurs and, also, are in leadership roles in
corporate and academia, they may significantly contribute to the
growth of the institute. Creation of a strong alumni connect is
Important.

iv) We may focus on one or two niche areas out of the priorities
charted out in the New Education Policy 2020, where we can
excel at the national level.

Agenda Item No. 6

Proposal to establish a School of Design from Academic Year 2026-
27

Following up a suggestion of Prof. Tridip Suhrud in the previous Board
meeting regarding setting up of a school of design, Professor
Bandyopadhyay briefed the Board about the programs (the fee structure,
duration, intake etc.) offered by other universities and institutes in India,
and especially in Gujarat. He feels that DAU can create its niche in the
design education space by offering programs aligned with emerging
technologies. He requested the board to set up a Task Force for
exploring the proposal of Prof. Suhrud.

One of the Board members informed the Board about the unique design
programs offered by some of reputed universities in US. The member
was requested to identify and give a list of such unique and tech-based
design programmes offered by such universities to help planning,

Another Board member suggested to look into the design thinking
programme offered by the ATLAS-ISDI School of Design and Innovation
in Mumbai as a part of the overall analysis in identifying the relevant
design programs for the school.

The Board agreed to the proposal to set up a Task Force in consultation
with Prof. Tridip Suhrud and Shri Shrikant Kulkarni. Shri Nikhil Dalal, a
board member accepted the invitation to be a member of the proposed
task force. The Board authorized Prof Bandyopadhyay to move ahead in
the matter and submit the recommendations of the task force to the
Board for further consideration.

Agenda Item No. 7:
Consideration of the draft annual report for the year 2023-24

The digital copy of draft annual report was circulated to the members
before the meeting for their kind perusal. The same was presented
before the Board for approval. The Board was informed about some
minor editing in progress like inserting appropriate photographs and
adding a few relevant information in the report.

The Board approved it in principle and requested to share the final

version of the report to the members for their observations an
suggestions if any, before publishing the same.

Agenda Item No. 8:

Ratification of the decision taken by the Chairman Finance
Committee to approve Audited Annual Accounts for the financial
year 2023-24.

The Board noted that there is no adverse remarks or observations by the
auditors. Also the members noted the major financial highlights of the
audited accounts. The Board ratified the decision of the Chairman
Finance Committee and approved the annual accounts for financial year
2023-24.

Agenda Item No. 3:
Presentation by the Director, School of Law

Prof. Avinash Dadhich, founding Director of School of Law, presented a
report on major activities and events conducted by the School since July
2024. He also presented the detailed road-map mentioning the planned
offerings of unique and industry specific law programmes.

The Board noted the proposed commencement of full time as well as
blended LLM Programs, PhD programs (full time and part-time), industry
specific executive education programmes and PG Diplomas for legal
practitioners as well for others working in public as well as private
sectors from the academic year 2025-26.

The Board appreciated the objectives, approaches and the road-map
prepared by the School and accorded in-principle approval to offer the
above mentioned programmes fram the academic year 2025-26.

Agenda Item No. 10:

Proposal to revise semester-wise tuition fees for all our academic
programmes and Hostel fees.

The Board perused the background note of the agenda item and
approved the proposal to convene a meeting of the Finance-Committee

to suggest revisions in the programme-wise tuition fees for the AY 2025-
26, to be submitted for further consideration of the Appellate Committee
on COE, Education Department, Government of Gujarat.

Agenda Item No. 11:

Presentation on planning of Silver Jubilee celebration of the
Institute

Professor Sanjay Srivastava, convenor of the committee constituted for
the silver jubilee celebration informed the Board that on August 6, 2025,
DA-IICT will complete its 25 years of existence. The institute plans to
start a round the year celebration from August 6, 2025. He made
presentation before the Board about the activities and events planned to
be carried out throughout the year.

The Board appreciated the thoughtful planning of activities and events
and took note of the same.

Agenda Item No. 12:
Noting of the new faculty members joined during the last year.

The Board noted the details and profiles of the new faculty members
joined during one-year period.

The meeting ended with a vote of thanks to the Chair.

Agenda Item No. 2

Consideration of the recommendations of a Committee appointed for the
proposed Restructuring of the Academic Governance

The Director set up a Committee to review and propose changes to the existing academic
governance model for greater decentralization of powers and responsibilities.

Professor Sanjay Srivastava, Convener of the said Committee and a member of the
Council, made a presentation with a background note that the Institute has grown and

are

diversified its program offerings; it was felt that the decision-making processes were
getting stretched. Thus, a need was felt to thoroughly re-examine the existing structures
and propose changes to bring faculty participation and decentralized decision-making,
While the Committee's other recommendations are still fine-tuned, the report is on
creating faculty clusters aligned with conventional academic domains and a soft-
departmental structure (called Area).

The Council deliberated on the proposal. After incorporating the suggestions of the
members and a few details in the report, the Council accepled the recommendations of
the Gommittee and requested the Director to implement them. The final document of
the report, as approved by the Council, is attached as Annexure |,

Agenda Item No. 3

Consideration of the recommendations of the Committee appointed for the review
of the PhD Programme at DA IICT.

Convener of the above-referred Committee and a member of the Council, Professor
Sunitha V., briefed the Council that the Director constituted the Commitiee for a
comprehensive review of the Ph.D. program to identify the probable causes for the
decrease in applications and admissions over the last few years, as well as other
academic concerns like allocation of supervisor, coursework, sell-study course,
comprehensive examination, etc., and suggest corrective measures to address the
concerns. Professor Sunitha V made a detailed presentation betore the Council,
hightighting the concerns and the recommendations made by the said Committee. Il was
clanfied that no changes have been proposed in the existing processes and policies
related to the Ph.D. Program, except for the following matters discussed in the meeting.

The Council perused all the concerns and gave its feedback on some of the Committee's
recommendations. Following are the suggested action poinis to be considered for
implementation,

i) In the part-time category of the applicants, there are two further
categories. One category of candidates is the sponsored candidates
from the organizations having MoU! Agreement with the Institute, and
the other is the external candidates fram the organizations not having
MoWAgreement with the Institute. The Council accepted the
recommendation of the Committee to introduce a system by which both
the categories of the part-time candidates will be required to interact with
the prospective supervisors about the area of the candidate's research
problam/statement and submit a letter of recommendation (at the time
of applying for admission) from the prospective supervisor about the
sultability’compatibility of the supervisor with the research area of the
candidate. Sponsored category candidates shall continue to submil a
no-objection certificate from the sponsoring organization at the time al
applying, as per the existing nonmns.

b)

ii}

il)

For exfarnal part-time candidates, a minimum of two years of relevant
experience will be introduced as one of the eligibility criteria for applying.

The Council accepted the proposal to waive the entrance examination
condition for all part-time candidates, whether they are sponsored or
external.

Concerning the full-lima candidates, the Council accepted the
recommendation of the Committee to waive the requirement of
candidates to appear in the entrance examination if they fulfill any of the
following conditions.

Valid GATE/NET qualification (no condition of the minimum score of 500, etc.)

Awarded with the MS by RESEARCH of an IITAISe/IIIT.

Awarded with an MTech from DA-WCT with 8.0 and above CPI.

sélected for DST-INSPIRE/NBHMJEST/PMAF/USG/CSIR/Visweshvaraya PhD
Scholarship.

)

—

Existing provisions for allocating a Supervisor, only alter clearing the
comprehensive examination, are to be replaced by the allocation of a
(prospective) Supervisor at the time of admissions for the pari-lime
candidates. The full-time scholars will find the Supervisor only alter
Semester | but before appearing for the comprehensive examination's
Part ll (literature survey.

With respect to the existing condition of the Course Work is to be
completed before appearing for the Comprehensive Examination (same
condition for part-time as well as full-lime candidates), the Council
accepted the proposal of the Cammittee for separate conditions for the
part-time and full-time candidates, as per the following details.

For part-time scholars, course credit requirements shall be compulsory
and continued without strictly adhering to the minimum attendance
requirements. They will complete all coursework before registering for
the Synopsis. They should register for at least three courses at the 5XX
and/or 74% level during Semesier!. Courses al 1XX, 2XX, XX, or 6X
shall not be counted towards course credit requiramenis.

For the full-time scholars, they will be required to complete at least two
‘Area’ level Core courses at the Graduate level for Semester | (which
shall be offered in a modular format with multiple instructors), as
Suggested by the Area Committee (in place of courses suggested by the
Mentors). They will complete all coursework before registering for the
synopsis. They should register for at least three courses at the 5XX

and/or 7XX level during Semester |. Courses at 1XX, 2XX, 3XX, or 6XX
shall not be counted towards course credit requirements.

vi) In the existing system, a scholar can do self-study course/s in any
semester. The Council accepted the recommendation of the Committee
that a Ph.D. scholar cannot take any self-study course in the first
semester, The scholar cannot register for multiple sell-study courses
under the same faculty. It was further clarified during the discussion that
the two self-study courses aré as per the prescribed credit requirements,
but a scholar can do more than two self-study courses for knowledge
enhancement.

vil) For the Comprehensive Examination for the part-time scholars, the
Council accepted the recommendation of ihe Commitiee to replace the
existing written examination with the new requirement of proposing a
Research Problem/Statement by the scholar to be approved by the
Research Commitiee which the Evaluation Committee will, later on,
evaluate with a maximum limit of two attempts to clear it. The first
attempt shall be semester II at ihe earliest.

Concerning the Comprehensive Examination for the full-time scholars, the
Council accepted the recommendation of the Committee that part 1, i.e., the
written examination part, will consist of two Core Courses based on the
syllabus-contents of the Area Core, and part Il, i.e., Literature Survey
Examination will be by a presentation open fo public and the Evaluaton
Commitiee. The scholar must clear both the parts satisfactorily, with a
maximum limit of two attempts for each part.

Agenda Item No.4

Ratification of a decision by the Director, on behalf of the Council, on the duration
of the Ph.D. Fellowship and the stipend amount.

The Council ratified the decision taken by the Director to pay the per month Ph.D,
Fellowship with effect from April 2024, an amount of As. 35,000/ for the first two years,
by replacing the existing two slabs of the Fellowship of As. 28,000/ for the first year and
Rs. 31,000/ after ihe passing of comprehensive examination for the second and third
year,

it was clarified that for the first two years, the Ph.O. scholar will get a fellowship of Rs.
35,000/- per month (without the condition of passing the comprehensive examination),
and from the third year and up to the fifth year, the scholar will be paid the Fellowship of
Rs. 40,000/ per month.

The meeting ended with a vote of thanks to the Chair.

recommendations of the previous meeting and informed thal there were no changes
in the draft minutes circulated earlier. With a correction to include Professor
Madhumita Mazumdar’s name in the list of attendees, the CounciLeonfirmed the
minutes of the 40th meeting. poe

Agenda ltem No. 2

Noting of Government of Gujarat Notification Declaring the University as a
Centre of Excellence (CoE)

The Executive Registrar informed the Council thal the Government of Gujarat has
confenrad CoE status upon the top ten technical and professional institutions in
Gujarat. DA-IICT has been awarded this recognition for the second consecutive
term, now extended to six academic years (2025-26 to 2030-31), unlike the three-
yoar term in the first cycle.

One major privilege in this cycle is the increased intake under {he management
quota in the BTech and MTech programs—irom 67% to 75%. Accordingly, the State
Government will allot only 25% of the intake through its centralized admission
process.

The Council took note of the development with appreciation for the collective efforts
led by (he Deans and Director General.

Agenda Item No. 3
Recommendations of the Board of Studies for the MDes Programs

Professors Anirban Dutla Gupta and Anupam Rana made a delailed presentation on
hwo key recommendabons:

i. A comprehensive review and revision of the MDes in Communication Design
curriculum, effective from AY 2025-26.

i. Introduction of a new specialization—MDes in Intelligent User Experience
Design (iUXD), effective from AY 2025-26, with a market-oriented and innovative
curmculum structure.

The Council appreciated the thorough planning, involvement of external experts, and

academic rigor in the proposals and approved the recommendations of the Board of
Studies

Agenda Item No. 4
Ratification and Approval of Academic Calendar 2025-26

The Council noted and approved the decision of the Director General to announce
the academic calendar for the Academic Year 2025-26.

| 3 \e Dota

284

Agenda Item No. 5

Proposal for Revision in Policy on Course Repetition for the grade
improvement or backlog, and Introduction of Summer Courses for PG
Students

Professor Tapas Maiti presented the rationale behind the proposed policy changes.
The Council made the following observations:

i. Insert a clause in the grade improvement policy allowing the best of the two
altempts to be considered.

ii. Address inconsistency in the grade requirements for UG (5 CPI) and PG (6. CPI)
students for backlog/grade improvement. The current proposal includes CD grade in
PG eligibility—this requires further review to align UG and PG norms.

ii. Gonsider offering MOOC courses (Massive Open Online Courses) during the
summer for PG students—particularly useful since many will be on internships.
These may inciude core and elective courses, with or without a cap on the number of
DOUPSES.

The Council authorized the Director General to further deliberate on these points and
finalize the revised policy.

Agenda Item No. 6
Faculty Recruited (January — June 2025)

The Council noted with appreciation the recruitment of nine new faculty members
during this period, as per the report presented.

Tabled Agenda Item

Agenda Item No. 7
Extension of Academic Timeline for PhD Scholar Pramod Tripathi

The Council ratified the Director General's decision—based on RPS Committee
recommendations—to grant an extension up to Autumn (December) 2025 to PhD
scholar Pramod Tripathi (201721003) to complete his academic raquirements,
including the PhD synopsis exam, in view of personal hardships faced during the
pandemic.

Agenda Item No. 3
Recommendations of the Board of Studies for PhD in Law = School of Law

Professor Nisheet Upadhyay presented the proposal, draft regulations, and
recommendations of the Board of Siudies for the PhO in Law program.

The Council approved, in principle, the introduction of the PhD in Law program from
AY 2025—26, subject to incorporation of minor suggestions from the Council.

The drat regulations were subsequently sent to Professor (Dr) William Nunes,
Gujarat National Law University, Gandhinagar, for review and integration of Council
observations.

Annexure ; Approved PhD in Law Regulations, after required revisions,

The meeting concluded with a vote of thanks to the Chair.

DHIRUBHA! AMBANI UNIVERSITY, GANDHINAGAR

MINUTES OF THE TWENTY-THIRD MEETING OF THE FINANCE COMMITTEE

The 23™ meeting of the Finance Committee was held on 30" January 2025 in a hybrid
mode in the Board Room of the Institute.

The following were present:

|) Professor Tathagata Bandyopadhyay, Director General, in Chair
7) Shri Shrikant Kulkarni, Member

4) Shri Umesh Dalal, Member

4) Professor Maniklal Das

Shri Shrikant Kulkarni and Shri Umesh Dalal, Members, participated in the meeting,
through the video conference mode.

The following items were discussed:

ltem No. 1

To propose revisions to our existing fee structure for all the academic
programmes and the hostel fees for the students to be admitted in academic
year 2025-26

Background Note:

The Education Department, Government of Gujarat. vide its notification dated 4th
January 2022 has declared seven private universities in the State of Gujarat as Centre
of Excellence (COE) for three years. We are one of the seven
universities, Subsequently, by a notification dated 7th March 2023, the COEs are
exempted from certain provisions of the Gujarat Professional Technical Educational
Colleges or Institutions (Regulation of Admissions and Fixation of Fees) Act 2007, As
one of the conditions of the said notification, COEs are required to notify/declare the
fee structure for the professional programs (BE-BTech, ME-MTech for us) to the
Appellate Committee, the proposed fee structure for the next academic year. As per
one of the provisions, if the AC within three weeks of receipt of the declaration finds
that the proposed fee structure substantially constitutes profiteering, the declaration
shall be referred back with comments to enable such institution to modify the fee
structure accordingly and shall not implement the proposed fee structure.

Accordingly, for the last AY 2024-25, after the due internal process, declared and
informed the Appellate Committee (AC) about our revised fees for the BTech and
MTech Programmes vide our letter dated Sth February 2024 (copy allache

vide its letter 14th August 2024 approved our proposed fee struc

programs (copy attached). A siatement showing the fee structure (increase in
comparison to the previous year) of our all academic programs and hostel fees for the
AY 2024-25 is also attached for reference.

A comparative statement of fees for ihe AY 2024-25 by the five COEs for their Tech
Program, including DAIICT, is attached herewith for your kind perusal. In the said
statement, from the current websiles of Ahmedabad University and POEU, we have
aiso mentioned the proposed fee for their BTech Program, published for the next AY
2025-26 admissions.

Matter for consideration:

The Finance Committee, as per the directives given by the Board of Governors in its
18th January 2025 meeting, is requested to consider and propose revisions to our
existing fee structure for all the programs for the students to be admitted in AY 2025-
26, and also for the existing batches of the students.

Resolution:

The Committee was appraised with the relevant Notification of Education Department,
Government of Gujarat and prescribed process described in it for the Centre of
Excellence institutions for the professional programmes offered by the Institutes. The
Committee was also appraised with the following documents.

a) Our last academic year's fees structure proposed to the Appellate Committee
vide our letter dated 9" February 2024.

b) Supporting documents to be attached with our proposal, for example, CA
certified statements (of the previous year) of income from the tuition fee and
expenditures of permissible expenses, as per the SOPs prescribed by the State
Goverment, along with all related schedules and annexures towards imparting
education in the BTech and MTech Programs and a consolidated statement for
all other Programs, to work out per student actual expenditures incurred and
per student income received by the institute.

c) Appellate Committee's Order dated 14" August 2024 approving our proposal
to increase the tuition fees in the professional programmes (BTech and MTech).

d) Comparative statement of tuition fees charged by prominent COEs during the
last AY and the fees proposed by some of them for the next A’ 2025-26,

After dua deliberation and examining the above documents, the Committee
recommended the following fees for all our programs, per semester, for the students
to be admitted in academic year 2025-26.

For the MTech Program proposed tuition fee, it was recommended to increase the
teaching assistantship (stipend) from the existing Rs. 12,500/ to Rs. 15,000) per
month. By this, the proposed annual tuition fees paid by the MTech students, Le.
Rs.1.80,000/ will be fully repaid by the institute to the MTech students by way of
teaching assistantship stipend amounts to them,

Sr | Name of the | Per Semester | Proposed Revisions per Semester
Programme tuition fees for | for AY 2025-26 (Rs.)

As the meeting was called for the above matter only, there were no further agenda
item/s, and the meeting ended with a vote of thanks to the Chair.

DHIRUBHAI AMBANI UNIVERSITY

Gandhinagar

MINUTES OF THE

TWENTY-FOURTH MEETING OF THE FINANCE COMMITTEE

The 24" meeting of the Finance Committee was held on 27" March 2025 in the
Boardroom of the Director General's office and the following members were

present.
The following were present:
1. Professor Tathagata Bandyopadhyay, in Chair

Professor Maniklal Das, Member

Shri Shrikant Kulkarni, Member, through video conference

Se woN

Shri Umesh Dalal, Member, through video conference

Non-member Secretary: Shri Siddharth Swaminarayan

Invitees: 1. Shri Dashank Shah, Dy. CFO Finance and Taxation

2. Smt Divyam Mishra, Dy. CFO Accounts

The following items were discussed:

291

Agenda Item 1:
Confirmation of Minutes

The minutes of the meeting held on 30° January 2025 were confirmed by all

members.
Agenda Item 2:
Action Taken Report

+ The Executive Registrar provided an update on the tuition fee
recommended by the Committee in its 23 meeting. Subsequent to the
meeting, we proposed tuition fees for the professional programs (BTech
and MTech) to the Appellate Committee of the Education Department.
The tuition fee for the BTech Program was proposed at Rs. 1,87,500/-per
semester. On receipt of informal feedback from the Department, a
revised proposal was sent to the Appellate Committee mentioning the
per semester tuition fee of Rs. 1,78,500/- for the BTech Program. The
matter is still under consideration of the Appellate Committee. This has

been informed to our Board of Governors, also.

+ The Committee took note that during the (last academic year) 2024-25
admissions, the Institute had announced (in the admissions brochures
and website) to the applicants of all programs that there will be an
increase in the tuition fees in the range of 8-10% (to adjust the
inflationary impact). The Committee deliberated on the matter and
checked the practices adopted by some of the reputed institutions,
including other COEs, and authorized the Director General to check the

rate of inflation during the last year and announce the appropriate

increase in the fees for the 2024-25 batch admitted students in all

Programs.

Agenda Item 3:
Revised Estimates of 2024-25 and Budget Estimates for 2025-76

The Committee examined the head-wise actual expenditures and income for
the audited accounts of FY 2023-24, BE of FY 2024-25, actual and revised
estimates for the FY 2024-25, variance analysis made for the said F¥s, and
examined proposed BE of 2025-26 in details and made the following

observations and suggestions.

Estimated Revenue Receipts:

a) Give effect to the proposed (inflationary) increase in the tuition fees
of all the programs students admitted in the 2024-25 batch.

b) Give effect of income of tuition fees from MSc Agriculture Analytics
Program, as itis decided to continue the said program for AY 2025-26 and
thereafter,

c) Give effect of estimated income of tuition fees fram newly
introduced MDes specialization in Intelligent User Experience Design from
AY 2025-26,

d) Revisit the calculation of interest income from Investments, i.e.
projected cash flow, and prospective interest rates, and work out more

realistic income from the investments.

293

Estimated Capital Budget:

a) For the new construction projects, i.e. construction of one more floor
on New Boys Hostel (Rs.9 crores) and the construction of New Canteens
(Rs.6 crores), the Committee suggested exploring the possibilities of
getting the funds through the CSR and or donations.

b) In the matter of procurement of the new desktops (ICT budget) to
replace the old desktops in the teaching labs, the Committee suggested
exploring the possibilities of introducing a provision of bringing own
laptops by the students at the time of admissions and gradually dispose-
off the ald desktops. In case, the existing old desktops in the particular
labs are good to continue with for one more year, then we may continue
with the same desktops and introduce the laptop policy from the next
academic year for the new admissions.

c) In the matter of the installation of additional solar panels for
generating 500 KWp power, the Committee issued directives to expedite
the procurement process as the same item was not executed during the
last FY. Electricity expenses were suggested to be reviewed in view of the
additional 500 KWp solar panels to be installed,

d) In the Estate Department's budgetary proposals, the Committee
suggested including budgetary provisions for the renovation of the

toilet/washroom blocks in the SAC | building.

Subject to considering the above mentioned observations, the Committee

approved the budget estimates for the FY 2025-26.

With the approval of the Chair, the following item was presented for noting of

the Committee.

Agenda Item 4:
Noting of the CSR funding received by the Institute

The Committee noted that since October 2023, the Institute has received CSR

funding/grants for the following two major activities, as continuing projects.

i) CSR Activity A: Promotion of education through the
transformation of the university into a multi-disciplinary university by
the establishment of Higher Education Institutions in other disciplines.
ii) CSR Activity B: Promotion of education by installing new state-
of-the-art IT equipment, teaching aids to upgrade/replace old

equipment, etc,

The Committee noted that the following four companies provided funding for

the above two purposes, from October 2023 to mid-March 2025.

i} Rosa Power Supply Company Ltd
ii} Dhursar Solar Power Private Ltd
iti) Sasan Power Ltd

iv) DS Toll Road Ltd

The Committee was appraised with a statements of consolidated accounts of

CSR funding during the above-mentioned period.

The Committee noted that the university has received a total of Rs.872 lakhs
funding from the above four companies for CSR Activity A, out of which till
date we have utilized Rs, 277.64 lakhs. Similarly, for CSR Activity B, the
university received Rs.127.61 lakhs, and out of which, to date, we have utilized
Rs. 118.56 lakhs.

The meeting ended with a vote of thanks to the Chair.

Annexure XXXI

otrategies to review
academic programs

303

Annexure XX

Strategies to review_Academic Programme

= The Institute has adopted a mechanism to review the curriculum of UG
programme once in every 5 years, and the curriculum of PG programme
once in every 3 years.

* Curriculum development and revision for all programs goes through a
process that begins with the constitution of a curriculum committee
headed by a senior faculty under the guidance of Board of Studies.

» Any new curriculum design committee consists of internal faculty, domain
experts, external experts from academia and industry.

= The committee discusses and comes out with a draft curriculum which
is then discussed in the larger forum of faculty body.

= On a notional approval from the faculty body the final draft of the
curriculum or the revision is again subjected to a brain storming process in
the presence of experts from the academia and industry.

* The final draft curriculum or suggested revision is then put up to Board of
Studies, which then forwards, with necessary recommendations, the
draft to Academic Council of the Institute for final approval.

= Once the Academic Council approves the curriculum, it goes for
implementation in the academic programme.

In our BTech and MTech programmes, the curriculum has a good degree of
flexibility in terms of electives, internships, and projects, For each course,
the pre-requisite course(s) and Jlearming objectives are clearly
stated based on which the instruction plan and assessment plan are
also well-defined for most of the courses. Course-wise feedback is
taken from students at least once in a semester. In some
courses, the instructor takes course feedback multiple times,
namely, at the beginning of the course, mid-semester feedback,
and end-semesterfeedback. The students’ feedback is taken imo
account for the susequent updating/upgrading the course
delivery mechanism, and for the course allocation in se
sessions.

The process followed in the curriculum induction or revision in our programme
is depicted below:

Other Information with
Achievements

DA-IICT Gandhinagar achieved MAAC Grade A+ Accreditation in 2024, recognizing its excellence in
higher education,

Awarded the status of "Center of Excellence’ by the Government of Gujarat, highlighting its leadership in
research and innovation. In the first cycle awarded for three years from January O04, 202% till academic
year 2024-25. In the second cycle awarded for a period of six academic years ie. 2025-26 to 2030-31.

Five Star Institution in the category of Universities in the Gujarat State Institutional Ratings Framewark
(GSIRF), reaffirming institutional excellence for the years 2023 and 7024,

Ranked No. ‘1 under the IRF University Ranking 2023 for overall State Private Universities, a significant
milestone for DA-ICT.

Granted the Certificate of Nodal Coordination in 7018 for exceptional collaboration and leadership in

coordination efforts.

Awarded a certificate by the Gandhinagar Corporation Swachh Survekshan in 2071 for contributing to
cleanliness and sustainability initiatives.

Food Safety and Standards Authority of India (FSSAJ) has awarded Five Star ratings to the Institute as
the Eat Right Campus for the period of 15 May 2025 to 14 May 2027.
